Homological systems and bocses
R. Bautista, E. Pérez, L. Salmerón

Abstract
We show that, up to Morita equivalence, any finite-dimensional algebra with a suitable homological system, admits an exact Borel subalgebra. This generalizes a theorem by Koenig, Külshammer, and Ovsienko, which holds for quasi-hereditary algebras. Our proof follows the same general scheme proposed by these authors, in a more general context: we associate a differential graded tensor algebra with relations, using the structure of A ∞ A_{\infty} -algebra of a suitable Yoneda algebra, and use its category of modules to describe the category of filtered modules associated to the given homological system.

†

2010
Mathematics Subject Classification
:
16E45, 16E30, 16G10, 16G20.
†
†

Keywords and phrases
: differential tensor
algebra, ditalgebra, bocs, homological systems,
standardly filtered modules, exact Borel subalgebras.

1
Introduction

We denote by
k
a fixed algebraically closed ground field. For every
k
-algebra or any bimodule over given
k
-algebras we consider, we assume that the field
k
acts centrally on them.

Given a
k
-algebra
\Lambda
, we denote by
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
the category of left
\Lambda
-modules and by
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
its full subcategory of finitely generated
\Lambda
-modules.

We recall that a
preordered set

({\cal P},\leq)
is a non-empty set
{\cal P}
equipped with a relation
\leq
such that
i\leq i
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
and such that, whenever we have
i,j,s\in{\cal P}
with
i\leq j
and
j\leq s
, we also have
i\leq s
. Two elements
i,j\in{\cal P}
are
equivalent
iff
i\leq j
and
j\leq i
. In this case, we write
i\sim j
.

Remark 1.1
.

Let
\overline{{\cal P}}={\cal P}/\sim
be the set of equivalence
classes of
{\cal P}
modulo the equivalence relation
\sim
. For any
i\in{\cal P}
, denote by
\overline{i}
its equivalence class. Then,
\overline{\cal P}
is a partially ordered set with the relation defined by
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}
iff
i\leq j
.

We recall the following terminology from
[
13
]
,
in the particular context we are interested in.

Definition 1.2
.

Given a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
, a
(finite) homological system
({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
consists of
a finite preordered set
({\cal P},\leq)
and
a family of pairwise non-isomorphic indecomposable finite-dimensional
\Lambda
-modules
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
satisfying the following two conditions:

1.

{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\not=0
implies
i\leq j
;

2.

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\not=0
implies
i\leq j
and
i\not\sim j
.

Given such an homological system, we write
\Delta:=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\Delta_{i}
and denote by
{\cal F}(\Delta)
the full subcategory of
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
consisting of the trivial module
0
and all those
M\in\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
which admit a
\Delta
-filtration
, that is a filtration of submodules

0=M_{t}\subseteq M_{t-1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq M_{1}\subseteq M_{0}=M

such that
M_{j}/M_{j+1}
is isomorphic to some module in
\{\Delta_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
, for each
j\in[0,t-1]
.

The following special homological systems will be relevant for us.

Definition 1.3
.

A finite homological system
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
for a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
is called
admissible
if
\Lambda\in{\cal F}(\Delta)
and the number of isomorphism classes of indecomposable projective
\Lambda
-modules coincides with the cardinality of
{\cal P}
.

An admissible homological system
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
will be called
strict
if the following condition is satisfied:

\hbox{For each }n\geq 1,\hbox{ we have that }{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\not=0\hbox{ implies }i\leq j\hbox{ and }i\not\sim j.

Proposition 1.4
.

Let
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
be an admissible homological system for some finite-dimensional algebra
\Lambda
. Denote by
{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}
a fixed set of representatives of the isoclasses of the indecomposable projective
\Lambda
-modules. Then, we can index
{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}
with
{\cal P}
in such a way that each
P_{i}\in{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}
is the projective cover of
\Delta_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
and, moreover,
there is an exact sequence

\begin{matrix}0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&Q_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&P_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Delta_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0,\end{matrix}

in
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
such that the module
Q_{i}
admits a
\Delta
-filtration with factors of the form
\Delta_{j}
, with
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
.

Proof.

From
[
13
]
(3.16), we know that
{\cal F}(\Delta)
is closed under direct summands, so every indecomposable projective
\Lambda
-module
P
belongs to
{\cal F}(\Delta)
. For each one of these indecomposable projectives
P
we can choose a
\Delta
-filtration

0=M_{t}\subseteq M_{t-1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq M_{1}\subseteq M_{0}=P

with
P/M_{1}\cong\Delta_{\sigma(P)}
, for some index
\sigma(P)\in{\cal P}
. So, there is a short exact sequence

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M_{1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}\Delta_{\sigma(P)}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

so
{\rm top}(P)\cong{\rm top}(\Delta_{\sigma(P)})
and
g
is the projective cover of
\Delta_{\sigma(P)}
. So, given two non-isomorphic indecomposable projectives
P
and
P^{\prime}
, their tops
{\rm top}(P)
and
{\rm top}(P^{\prime})
are not isomorphic, so the modules
\Delta_{\sigma(P)}
and
\Delta_{\sigma(P^{\prime})}
are not isomorphic. Since the cardinality of the set of isomorphism classes of the indecomposable projectives coincides with the cardinality of
{\cal P}
, the map
P\mapsto\Delta_{\sigma(P)}
determines a bijection between
{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}

and the set
\{\Delta_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
. Then, we can index with
{\cal P}
the set
{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}
in such a way that we have short exact sequences

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{i}}}P_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g_{i}}}\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

with
P_{i}
indecomposable projective, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

From
[
13
]
(3.12) and
[
13
]
(3.4), for each
i\in{\cal P}
, there is an exact sequence

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}U_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{u_{i}}}X_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{v_{i}}}\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

such that:
the module
X_{i}\in{\cal F}(\Delta)
is indecomposable and satisfies
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(X_{i},Y)=0
, for all
Y\in{\cal F}(\Delta)
; and
the module
U_{i}\in{\cal F}(\Delta)
is filtered by modules in
\{\Delta_{j}\mid j\geq i\hbox{ and }j\not\sim i\}
.

Since
P_{i}
is projective, there is a morphism
s_{i}:P_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}X_{i}
such that
v_{i}s_{i}=g_{i}
. Moreover, we have a pull-back diagram

\begin{matrix}0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&M_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&E_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\pi_{i}}}&X_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0\\
&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle id_{M_{i}}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle h_{i}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle v_{i}$}}$\hss}&&\\
0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&M_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{i}}}&P_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g_{i}}}&\Delta_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0.\\
\end{matrix}

Since
{\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(X_{i},M_{i})=0
, the exact sequence of the first row in the preceding diagram splits, and there is a morphism
\sigma_{i}:X_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{i}
with
\pi_{i}\sigma_{i}=id_{X_{i}}
. The morphism
t_{i}:=h_{i}\sigma_{i}:X_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{i}
satisfies
g_{i}t_{i}=g_{i}h_{i}\sigma_{i}=v_{i}\pi_{i}\sigma_{i}=v_{i}
. Then, we have that

v_{i}=g_{i}t_{i}=v_{i}s_{i}t_{i}
and
g_{i}=v_{i}s_{i}=g_{i}t_{i}s_{i}
, or equivalently
v_{i}(id_{X_{i}}-s_{i}t_{i})=0
and
g_{i}(id_{P_{i}}-t_{i}s_{i})=0
, so
id_{X_{i}}-s_{i}t_{i}
and
id_{P_{i}}-t_{i}s_{i}
are not isomorphisms. Since the endomorphism algebras of
P_{i}
and
X_{i}
are local, we get that
s_{i}t_{i}
and
t_{i}s_{i}
are isomorphisms. From this, we get that
s_{i}
is an isomorphism and
P_{i}\cong X_{i}
.
∎

Definition 1.5
.

Assume that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is an admissible homological system for a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
and keep the index assignation imposed on
{\rm proj}_{\Lambda}
in (
1.4
).
Consider a non-zero
M\in\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and any projective resolution

P_{M}\hbox{ }:\hbox{ }\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{M}^{-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{M}^{0}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

of
M
. Then, for
i\in{\cal P}
,
we will say that the projective resolution
P_{M}
is
i
-bounded
iff it is finite and,
whenever
P_{j}
is a direct summand of
P_{M}^{-t}
, we have
\overline{j}\geq\overline{i}
for
t\geq 0
, with
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
if
t>0
.

Lemma 1.6
.

Assume that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is an admissible homological system for a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
. Then, for each
i\in{\cal P}
, the
\Lambda
-module
\Delta_{i}
admits an
i
-bounded minimal projective resolution
P_{\Delta_{i}}
of the form

P_{\Delta_{i}}\hbox{ }:\hbox{ }\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{i}}^{-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{i}}^{0}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0.

Proof.

Notice that if
i\in{\cal P}
is such that
\overline{i}
is maximal in
\overline{\cal P}
, in the sequence of (
1.4
), we get
Q_{i}=0
, so
\Delta_{i}\cong P_{i}
is projective and
\Delta_{i}
has a trivial
i
-bounded projective resolution
P_{\Delta_{i}}
with
P_{\Delta_{i}}^{0}=P_{i}
.

Observe that given
i\in{\cal P}
and an exact sequence
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0
in
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
,
where
M
admits an
i_{1}
-bounded projective resolution
P_{M}
and
N
admits an
i_{2}
-bounded projective resolution
P_{N}
, with
\overline{i_{1}},\overline{i_{2}}>\overline{i}
, then the horseshoe lemma yields an
i
-bounded projective resolution
P_{E}
of
E

such that:
P_{j}
direct summand of
P_{E}^{0}
implies that
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
.

An easy induction using the last observation, shows that whenever

E\in{\cal F}(\Delta)
has a
\Delta
-filtration with factors
\{\Delta_{i_{u}}\}_{u\in[1,s]}

with
\overline{i_{u}}>\overline{i}
for all
u\in[1,s]
, which have
i_{u}
-bounded projective resolutions
\{P_{\Delta_{i_{u}}}\}_{u\in[1,s]}
, with
P^{0}_{\Delta_{i_{u}}}=P_{i_{u}}
, we have an
i
-bounded projective resolution
P_{E}
of
E
such that:
P_{j}
direct summand of
P_{E}^{0}
implies that
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
.

Finally, fix
i\in{\cal P}
such that
\overline{i}
is not maximal in
\overline{\cal P}
and assume that
\Delta_{j}
admits an
i
-bounded
projective resolution
P_{\Delta_{j}}
with
P^{0}_{\Delta_{j}}=P_{j}
, for all
j\in{\cal P}
with
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
. Consider the exact sequence of (
1.4
) for
\Delta_{i}
. Then the preceding paragraph yields an
i
-bounded projective resolution
P_{Q_{i}}
for
Q_{i}
, such that:
P_{j}
direct summand of
P_{Q_{i}}^{0}
implies that
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
. Then, splicing this exact sequence with the projective resolution
P_{Q_{i}}
, we obtain the wanted

i
-bounded projective resolution of
\Delta_{i}

P_{\Delta_{i}}\hbox{ }:\hbox{ }\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{Q_{i}}^{-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{0}_{Q_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0.

Finally, any minimal projective resolution of
\Delta_{i}
is a direct summand of
P_{\Delta_{i}}
and, therefore, is also
i
-bounded.
∎

Recall from
[
13
]
the following.

Definition 1.7
.

Assume that
\Lambda
is a finite dimensional
k
-algebra, equipped with a preordered set of indexes
({\cal P},\leq)
for the family
\{P_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
of representatives of the
non-isomorphic indecomposable projective
\Lambda
-modules. For

i\in{\cal P}
, denote by
\Delta_{i}
the
i^{th}
-
standard
\Lambda
-module
, that is
\Delta_{i}:=P_{i}/T_{i}
, where

T_{i}:=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
\overline{j}\not\leq\overline{i}\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f)

Then, the algebra
\Lambda
is called a

prestandardly stratified algebra
if, for each
i\in{\cal P}
,
there is an exact sequence

\begin{matrix}0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&Q_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&P_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Delta_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0,\end{matrix}

in
\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
such that the module
Q_{i}
admits a
\Delta
-filtration with factors of the form
\Delta_{j}
, with
\overline{j}>\overline{i}
.
A prestandardly stratified algebra
\Lambda
, with preordered index set
({\cal P},\leq)
is called
standardly stratified
if
({\cal P},\leq)
is a partial order. A standardly stratified algebra
\Lambda
is
quasi-hereditary
iff
({\cal P},\leq)
is linearly ordered and
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i})\cong k
, for each
i\in{\cal P}
.

Remark 1.8
.

If
\Lambda
is a prestandardly stratified algebra with preorder
({\cal P},\leq)
and standard modules
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, then
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in I})
is an admissible homological system, see
[
13
]
(2.3). If, furthermore, the algebra
\Lambda
is quasi-hereditary, then the homological system
{\cal H}
is strict, see
[
12
]
.

The following statement is interesting because it assumes no requirement on the endomorphism algebras of the standard modules.

Corollary 1.9
.

Assume that
\Lambda
is prestandardly stratified with homological system of standard modules
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
, where
({\cal P},\leq)
is linearly ordered. Then the homological system
{\cal H}
is strict.

Proof.

In the exact sequence granted by (
1.4
) for
the standard module
\Delta_{i}
, we can assume that the epimorphism
P_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta_{i}
is the canonical projection from
P_{i}
onto
\Delta_{i}=P_{i}/T_{i}
, where
T_{i}=\sum_{f\in{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{j},P_{i});j>i}{\rm Im}\,(f)
.
From (
1.6
), we have an

i
-bounded projective resolution

P_{\Delta_{i}}\hbox{ }:\hbox{ }\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{i}}^{-2}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{-1}_{\Delta_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\nu_{i}}}\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

where
\nu_{i}
is the canonical projection.
Fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\not=0
. This implies that

{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}}^{-n},\Delta_{j})\not=0
. So, there is an indecomposable direct summand
P_{s}
of
P_{\Delta_{i}}^{-n}
, which must satisfy
i<s
, with
{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P_{s},\Delta_{j})\not=0
. Take any non-zero morphism
h:P_{s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta_{j}
. Since
P_{s}
is projective, there is some morphism
f:P_{s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{j}
with
\nu_{j}f=h
. If
s>j
, we have
\nu_{j}({\rm Im}\,(f))=0
, and
h=0
, which is not the case. So we have

i<s\leq j
.
∎

The study of the category
{\cal F}(\Delta)
in the mentioned quasi-hereditary case, and in more general settings, has been of great
interest in representation theory of algebras, see
[
13
]
and its references.

In this paper we generalize the main results of S. Koenig, J. Külshammer and S. Ovsienko proved in
[
9
]
for the quasihereditary case. Here we extend their arguments to arbitrary strict admissible homological systems. In the following lines we give the layout of the contents of this article.

After section 2, which contains technical remarks on graded duals and tensor products, we show in section 3 that the Yoneda algebra
A
of the
\Lambda
-module
\Delta
, associated to a strict homological system
{\cal H}
for a finite dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
, admits a strict structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra, see (
3.7
).
For this, we proceed as in
[
9
]
, we reelaborate Keller’s argument in
[
8
]
supported by Kadeishvili Theorem (see also Merkulov
[
14
]
), in our more general context.

In section 4, we construct the interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)=({\cal A}(\Delta),I)
, see (
9.2
) and (
4.19
), associated to the Yoneda
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A
of
\Delta
. Throughout the whole paper we use the language of ditalgebras (diferential tensor algebras) and their categories of modules, as in
[
3
]
, enriched with the possibility of considering “ditalgebras with relations” called here interlaced weak ditalgebras and their categories of modules denoted by
({\cal A},I)\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, see (
8.1
) and
[
1
]
. We prefer this to the alternative language of bocses, used in
[
9
]
, because it seems to ease many computations.

In section 5, 6 and 7, we review elementary properties of
A_{\infty}
-categories and review the
A_{\infty}
-categories
{\rm ad}(A)
,
{\rm cv}(A)
, and
{\rm tw}(A)
with some of their relationships. All this supports the work of section 8 which exhibits an equivalence of categories
{\cal F}(\Delta)\simeq H^{0}({\rm tw}(A))\simeq\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. This generalizes theorem (8.2) of
[
9
]
to the case of an arbitrary strict homological system. In their domain of study, the homological system is of standard modules over a quasi-hereditary algebra, and they obtain a directed bocs. This is no longer true in our case of a general strict homological system, but we still obtain a manageable strict interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
, see (
12.1
).

We study in section 10 and 11 some special kind of interlaced weak ditalgebras
\underline{\cal A}
. We equip their categories of modules
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
with a natural exact structure and then show that Burt-Butler theory on their right and left algebras still works, see
[
5
]
and
[
6
]
. All this is applied later to the interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
. So, its right algebra
\Gamma
admits a natural strict homological system
{\cal H}^{\prime}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
and, moreover, the algebra
\Gamma
is Morita equivalent to
\Lambda
.

In the last section,
we show that the Yoneda algebra associated to an admissible homological system
{\cal H}
admits a strict structure of

A_{\infty}
-algebra, as in (
3.8
), if and only if the homological system
{\cal H}
is strict, see (
12.8
).

Moreover, generalizing the argumentation of
[
9
]
,
we prove that any finite dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
with a strict homological system
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is Morita equivalent to a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Gamma
which admits a strict homological system

{\cal H}^{\prime}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}\}_{i\in P})
such that
\Gamma
has an exact Borel subalgebra
B
, which is regular and homological, as in the following definition (
1.14
). Moreover, there is an equivalence
\Omega:\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
such that
\Omega(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\cong\Delta_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. In the next lines we give some terminology required for this definition.

Remark 1.10
.

Let
B
be a basic finite-dimensional
k
-algebra.
Let us recall that
the (Gabriel) quiver
Q
of the algebra
B
is constructed as follows. Consider a splitting
B=S\oplus J
of the algebra
B
over its radical
J
, then
S
is a finite product of copies of the field
k
and we can write
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, where
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit of
B
as a finite sum of primitive orthogonal idempotents. So, we get that
\{P_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
, where
P_{i}=Be_{i}
, is a complete family of representatives of the isoclasses of the indecomposable projective
B
-modules, and
the family
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
, where
S_{i}=P_{i}/JP_{i}
is a complete family of representatives of the isoclasses of the indecomposable simple
B
-modules.
Then, by definition, the set of points of
Q
is
{\cal P}
and, given
i,j\in{\cal P}
, there are exactly
\dim_{k}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})
arrows from
i
to
j
in
Q
.

Consider the
preorder of precedence

\preceq
in the set
{\cal P}
determined by
i\prec j
if there is an arrow
i\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}j
in
Q
or, equivalently, if
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
.

Recall that
B
can be identified with a quotient
k(Q)/I
of the path algebra
k(Q)
by some admissible ideal
I
, where the idempotents
e_{i}
are identified with the classes of the trivial paths of
Q
. Then, we have for different
i,j\in{\cal P}
that
e_{j}Be_{i}\not=0
implies
i\prec j
.

If the algebra
B
is
directed
, that is if its quiver
Q
has no oriented cycle, then
\preceq
is a partial order on

{\cal P}
. In this case, we have
e_{i}Be_{i}=ke_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

The following lemma illustrates the preceding notions, but it is convenient to make some precisions first.

Remark 1.11
.

For any finite poset
(X,\leq)
, its
height map

h:X\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
is defined for minimal elements
x\in X
, as
h(x)=0
, and, inductively, for any non-minimal element
x\in X
, as

h(x):=\hbox{max}\{h(y)\mid y<x\}+1
.

Clearly, whenever we have
y<x
in
X
, we get
h(y)<h(x)
.

Remark 1.12
.

A linearization
(X,\leq^{L})
of a finite poset
(X,\leq)
is defined as follows. Consider the subsets
X_{t}:=\{i\in X\mid h(i)=t\}
, so
X=\biguplus_{t=1}^{s}X_{t}
, for some finite
s
. Then, consider for each
t\in[1,s]
any linear order
\leq^{t}
in the set
X_{t}
. Then, by definition, for any elements
i,j\in X
, we make
i\leq^{L}j
if
h(i)<h(j)
or (
h(i)=h(j)
and
i\leq^{t}j
).

Lemma 1.13
.

Assume that
B
is a directed finite-dimensional
k
-algebra and adopt the notations of (
1.10
). Then, the following holds.

1.

The triple
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\preceq,\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is a strict homological system for the directed algebra
B
.

2.

For each
i\in{\cal P}
, the
i^{th}
-standard
B
-module associated to the partially ordered set
({\cal P},\preceq)
is
\Delta_{i}=S_{i}
. Clearly, we have
{\rm End}_{B}(S_{i})\cong k
.

3.

For any linearization
\preceq^{L}
of
{\cal P}
with the partial order of precedence
\preceq
, we have that
B
is a quasi-hereditary algebra with homological system of standard modules
{\cal H}^{L}=({\cal P},\preceq^{L},\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
.

Proof.

(1): We already know that
({\cal P},\preceq)
is a finite partially ordered set and that
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a family of non-isomorphic indecomposable
B
-modules. Clearly,
{\rm Hom}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})=0
, unless
i=j
. By definition of the order of precedence
\preceq
, we have
that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
implies that
i\prec j
. Here, we have
\overline{\cal P}={\cal P}
and
\Lambda\in{\cal F}(\oplus_{i\in{\cal P}}S_{i})
, so
{\cal H}

is an admissible homological system for the algebra
B
.

Let us show that
{\cal H}
is strict. Assume that
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
and
consider the
i
-bounded projective resolution of
S_{i}

\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{S_{i}}^{-n-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ d^{-n}\ }}P_{S_{i}}^{-n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ d^{-n+1}\ }}P_{S_{i}}^{-n+1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\cdots\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{S_{i}}^{-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{d^{0}}}P_{S_{i}}^{0}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\varepsilon}}S_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

given by (
1.6
).
Then, for each
n\geq 1
, we have

P^{-n}_{S_{i}}\cong\bigoplus_{i\prec t}m_{i,t}P_{t},\hbox{ for some }m_{i,t}\geq 0.

In order to compute
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})
, apply the functor
{\rm Hom}_{B}(-,S_{j})
to the given projective resolution of
S_{i}
and then look at the
n^{th}
-homology space, which is a quotient of the space
Z_{n}(P_{S_{i}}^{-n},S_{j})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{S_{i}}^{-n},S_{j})
. From the given decomposition of
P_{S_{i}}^{-n}
, we see that this space is zero whenever
i\not\prec j
. Thus,
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
implies
i\prec j
, and
{\cal H}
is a strict homological system for the algebra
B
.

(2): Observe that if
i,j\in{\cal P}
are incomparable
in the poset
({\cal P},\preceq)
, then we have
{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})=e_{j}Be_{i}=0
. Therefore, we get

T_{i}=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
j\not\preceq i\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f)=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
i\prec j\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f)={\rm rad}(P_{i}),

and we obtain
\Delta_{i}=P_{i}/T_{i}=S_{i}
.

(3): By definition of the linearization
\preceq^{L}
, we have that
i\prec^{L}j
implies that
i\prec j
or
i,j
are
\preceq
-incomparable elements of
{\cal P}
. Then, we obtain

T_{i}=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
i\prec j\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f)=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
i\prec^{L}j\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f)=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}f\in{\rm Hom}_{B}(P_{j},P_{i})\\
j\not\preceq^{L}i\end{matrix}}{\rm Im}\,(f).

Then, if
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
denotes the standard
B
-module associated to the linear order
\preceq^{L}
, we get
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}=S_{i}
,
for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

Since
i\prec j
implies
i\prec^{L}j
, the fact that
{\cal H}
is a strict homological system for
B
implies that
{\cal H}^{L}
is a strict homological system for
B
. Hence,
B
is a quasi-hereditary algebra.
∎

The following definition adapts slightly the definition of exact Borel subalgebra given in
[
4
]
(3.4).

Definition 1.14
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra with a strict homological system

{\cal H}^{\prime}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in P})
. Then, a subalgebra
B
of
\Gamma
is an
exact Borel subalgebra
if

1.

The finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
B
is directed and
we can index a complete family
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
of representatives of the isoclasses of the simple
B
-modules with the same index set
{\cal P}
. Thus (
1.13
) applies to
B
, in particular
B
is quasi-hereditary with simple standard modules.

2.

The right
B
-module
\Gamma
is projective.

3.

For each
i\in{\cal P}
, we have that
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\cong\Gamma\otimes_{B}S_{i}
.

The subalgebra
B
is called an
homological exact Borel subalgebra
if the morphisms

{\rm Ext}^{n}_{B}(M,N)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{B}M,\Gamma\otimes_{B}N)

induced by the tensor product functor
\Gamma\otimes_{B}-
are epimorphisms for
n\geq 1
and isomorphisms for
n\geq 2
.

The subalgebra
B
is called a
regular exact Borel subalgebra
if the morphisms

{\rm Ext}^{n}_{B}(S_{i},S_{j})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{B}S_{i},\Gamma\otimes_{B}S_{j})
induced by the tensor product functor
\Gamma\otimes_{B}-
are isomorphisms for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
i,j\in{\cal P}
.

2
Graded duals and tensor product

Let
S
be a fixed basic finite-dimensional semisimple
k
-algebra.
Recall the following basic isomorphism involving left duals of
S
-
S
-bimodules.

Lemma 2.1
.

Given an
S\hbox{-}S
-module
V
, we have the left dual
S
-
S
-bimodule
D(V):={\rm Hom}_{S}(\,_{S}V,\,_{S}S)
.
Then, given two
S
-
S
-bimodules
V
and
W
, where
W
is finitely generated as a left
S
-module,
there is a natural isomorphism of
S
-
S
-bimodules

\theta_{V,W}:D(W)\otimes_{S}D(V)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}D(V\otimes_{S}W)

satisfying that
\theta(\beta\otimes\alpha)[v\otimes w]=\alpha(v\beta(w))
,
for
\alpha\in D(V)
,
\beta\in D(W)
,
v\in V
, and
w\in W
.
Its inverse

\tau:D(V\otimes_{S}W)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}D(W)\otimes_{S}D(V)
can be described by

\tau(\gamma)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w^{*}_{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}
, where
(w_{i},w_{i}^{*})_{i\in[1,n]}
is a dual basis for the projective left
S
-module
W
,
\gamma\in D(V\otimes_{S}W)
, and
\gamma_{i}\in D(V)
is the morphism of left
S
-modules given by
\gamma_{i}(v)=\gamma(v\otimes w_{i})
, for
v\in V

and
i\in[1,n]
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that both,
\theta
and
\tau
are well defined maps and that
\theta
is a morphism of
S
-
S
-bimodules. For each
\gamma\in D(V\otimes_{S}W)
we have that

\theta\tau(\gamma)[v\otimes w]=\theta[\sum_{i}w^{*}_{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}](v\otimes w)=\sum_{i}\gamma_{i}(vw_{i}^{*}(w))=\sum_{i}\gamma(vw_{i}^{*}(w)\otimes w_{i})=\gamma(v\otimes\sum_{i}w_{i}^{*}(w)w_{i})=\gamma(v\otimes w)
, thus
\theta\tau=id
. Moreover, we have for each
i\in[1,n]
that

\theta(\beta\otimes\alpha)_{i}[v]=\theta(\beta\otimes\alpha)[v\otimes w_{i}]=\alpha(v\beta(w_{i}))=\beta(w_{i})\alpha[v]
and so
\theta(\beta\otimes\alpha)_{i}=\beta(w_{i})\alpha
. Therefore, we get

\tau\theta[\beta\otimes\alpha]=\sum_{i}w_{i}^{*}\otimes\theta(\beta\otimes\alpha)_{i}=\sum_{i}w_{i}^{*}\otimes\beta(w_{i})\alpha=\sum_{i}w_{i}^{*}\beta(w_{i})\otimes\alpha=\beta\otimes\alpha
. Then, we also have that

\tau\theta=id
and
\theta
is an isomorphism.

For the naturality of the isomorphism, it is clear that, given morphisms of
S
-
S
-bimodules
f:V\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}V^{\prime}
and
g:W\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}W^{\prime}
, the following squares commute

\begin{matrix}D(W)\otimes D(V^{\prime})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\theta}}&D(V^{\prime}\otimes W)\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle id_{D(W)}\otimes D(f)$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle D(f\otimes id_{W})$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow\\
D(W)\otimes D(V)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\theta}}&D(V\otimes W)\\
\end{matrix}\hbox{and}\begin{matrix}D(W^{\prime})\otimes D(V)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\theta}}&D(V\otimes W^{\prime})\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle D(g)\otimes id_{D(V)}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle D(id_{V}\otimes g)$}}$\hss}\\
D(W)\otimes D(V)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\theta}}&D(V\otimes W).\\
\end{matrix}

∎

Notation 2.2
.

By assumption, the
k
-algebra
S
is a finite product of copies of the field
k
. Consider the
decomposition of its unit
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
as a sum of primitive central orthogonal idempotents.

Given any
S
-
S
-bimodule
B
, an element
z\in B
is called
directed
iff
z\in e_{j}Be_{i}
, for some
i,j\in{\cal P}
. In this case, we write
s(z)=i
for its
source point
and
t(z)=j
for its
terminal point
.

In the following, we consider the algebra
S

as a graded
S
-
S
-bimodule concentrated at degree zero.

Definition 2.3
.

Let
B=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}B_{n}
be a graded
S
-
S
-bimodule.
Then, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
graded (left) dual
S
-
S
-bimodule

D(B)
is defined as

D(B)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}D(B)_{n},

where
D(B)_{n}:={\rm Hom}_{S}(B_{-n},S)=D(B_{-n})
, for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, we have

D(B)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{S}(B_{-n},S)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm GMod}\hbox{-}S}^{n}(B,S)={\rm Hom}_{{\rm GMod}\hbox{-}S}(B,S).

Given a homogeneous morphism of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules
f:A\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
of degree
|f|=d
,
we have the restriction morphism
f_{n}:A_{-n-d}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B_{-n}
which determines a morphism of
S
-
S
-bimodules

D(f_{n}):{\rm Hom}_{S}(B_{-n},S)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{S}(A_{-n-d},S).

Hence, we have the morphism
D(f)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}D(f_{n})
of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules

D(B)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{S}(B_{-n},S)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{S}(A_{-n-d},S)=D(A),

which is homogeneous with degree
|D(f)|=d=|f|
.

Remark 2.4
.

A graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
B=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}B_{n}
is called
locally finite
iff each
B_{n}
is finite-dimensional over
k
.
We will say that a graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
B
is
bounded below
(resp.
bounded above
) if there is some
\ell_{B}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
B_{n}=0
, for all
n<\ell_{B}
(resp. for all
n>\ell_{B}
).

Notice that
D(B)
is a graded locally finite bounded above (resp. bounded below)
S
-
S
-bimodule, whenever
B
is a graded locally finite bounded below (resp. bounded above)
S
-
S
-bimodule.

Moreover, if
A
and
B
are graded locally finite bounded below (resp. bounded above)
S
-
S
-bimodules, then the graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
A\otimes_{S}B
is locally finite and bounded below (resp. bounded above). Indeed, if
A
and
B
are bounded below by
\ell_{A}
and
\ell_{B}
, respectively then
A\otimes_{S}B
is bounded below by
\ell_{A}+\ell_{B}
:
Each homogeneous component
[A\otimes_{S}B]_{n}=\bigoplus_{s+t=n}A_{s}\otimes_{S}B_{t}
is a finite direct sum, because
A_{n-t}\otimes_{S}B_{t}\not=0
implies that
\ell_{B}\leq t\leq n-\ell_{A}
. If
n<\ell_{A}+\ell_{B}
and
A_{n-t}\otimes_{S}B_{t}\not=0
we get a contradiction. Thus, we get
[A\otimes_{S}B]_{n}=0
, for
n<\ell_{A}+\ell_{B}
, and then
A\otimes_{S}B
is bounded below.

We have the graded category
{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
(resp.
{\rm G\overline{b}}\hbox{-}S
) of graded locally finite bounded below (resp. bounded above)
S
-
S
-bimodules, with hom set

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S}(A,B)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{S\hbox{-}S}^{n}(A,B),

where
{\rm Hom}_{S\hbox{-}S}^{n}(A,B)
is the set of homogeneous morphisms of
S
-
S
-bimodules of degree
n
. Then, we have
the contravariant equivalence of graded categories

D:{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm G\overline{b}}\hbox{-}S.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
A,B\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
and consider the
bounded below locally finite graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
A\otimes_{S}B
.
Then, we have a canonical isomorphism of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules

D(B)\otimes_{S}D(A)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \theta_{A,B}\ \ }}D(A\otimes_{S}B)

such that
\theta_{A,B}(\beta\otimes\alpha)[a\otimes b]=\alpha(a\beta(b)),

for any homogeneous elements
\beta\in D(B)
,

\alpha\in D(A)
,
a\in A
, and
b\in B
. Its inverse is again an isomorphism of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules

D(A\otimes_{S}B)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \tau_{A,B}\ \ }}D(B)\otimes_{S}D(A).

Proof.

The morphism
\theta_{A,B}
maps, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the finite direct sum

[D(B)\otimes_{S}D(A)]_{n}=\bigoplus_{r+s=n}D(B)_{r}\otimes_{S}D(A)_{s}=\bigoplus_{r+s=n}{\rm Hom}_{S}(B_{-r},S)\otimes_{S}{\rm Hom}_{S}(A_{-s},S)

onto the direct sum

[D(A\otimes_{S}B)]_{n}={\rm Hom}_{S}([A\otimes_{S}B]_{-n},S)=\bigoplus_{r+s=n}{\rm Hom}_{S}(A_{-s}\otimes_{S}B_{-r},S)

with the direct sum of isomorphisms

\bigoplus_{r+s=n}\theta_{A_{-s},B_{-r}}
described in (
2.1
).
The latter can be applied because
B_{-r}
is a finitely generated left
S
-module.
∎

Definition 2.6
.

Consider the contravariant equivalence

\hat{D}:{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm G\overline{b}}\hbox{-}S,

defined by
\hat{D}(A)=D(A)^{op}
, the opposite
S
-
S
-bimodule of
D(A)
. Thus,
D(A)^{op}=D(A)
as vector spaces and, whenever
s,s^{\prime}\in S
, the action of
S
on
\hat{D}(A)
, denoted with a star
*
is given by
s*\beta*s^{\prime}=s^{\prime}\beta s
, for
\beta\in D(A)
. Moreover,
\hat{D}(f):\hat{D}(B)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(A)
has the same recipe than
D(f)
.

Then, for any
A,B\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
we have an isomorphism
of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules

\hat{D}(A)\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(B)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \hat{\theta}_{A,B}\ \ }}\hat{D}(A\otimes_{S}B)

such that
\hat{\theta}_{A,B}(\alpha\otimes\beta)[a\otimes b]=\alpha(a\beta(b)),

for any homogeneous elements
\beta\in\hat{D}(B)
,

\alpha\in\hat{D}(A)
,
a\in A
, and
b\in B
.

The map
\hat{\theta}_{A,B}
is obtained as the composition

D(A)^{op}\otimes_{S}D(B)^{op}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\zeta}}(D(B)\otimes_{S}D(A))^{op}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \theta_{A,B}\ \ }}D(A\otimes_{S}B)^{op}

where
\zeta
is the flip isomorphism
\alpha\otimes\beta\mapsto\beta\otimes\alpha
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Given two homogeneous morphisms
f:A\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A^{\prime}
and
g:B\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B^{\prime}
in
{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
, we have the homogeneous morphism
f\otimes g:A\otimes_{S}B\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A^{\prime}\otimes_{S}B^{\prime}
in
{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
.
Then, the following diagram commutes in
{\rm G\overline{b}}\hbox{-}S
:

\begin{matrix}\hat{D}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(B^{\prime})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hat{\theta}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}}}&\hat{D}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{S}B^{\prime})\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hat{D}(f)\otimes\hat{D}(g)$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle(-1)^{|f||g|}\hat{D}(f\otimes g)$}}$\hss}\\
\hat{D}(A)\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(B)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hat{\theta}_{A,B}}}&\hat{D}(A\otimes_{S}B).\\
\end{matrix}

Proof.

Take a typical homogeneous generator
\alpha\otimes\beta
of
\hat{D}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(B^{\prime})
, so
\alpha\in\hat{D}(A^{\prime})
and
\beta\in\hat{D}(B^{\prime})
are homogeneous, and take
a\otimes b\in A\otimes_{S}B
a typical homogeneous generator, so
a\in A
and
b\in B
are homogeneous.
Make
\Delta_{1}=(-1)^{|f||g|}[\hat{D}(f\otimes g)\hat{\theta}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(\alpha\otimes\beta)](a\otimes b)
, then we have

\begin{matrix}\Delta_{1}&=&(-1)^{|f||g|}[\hat{\theta}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(\alpha\otimes\beta)(f\otimes g)](a\otimes b)\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{|f||g|+|g||a|}\hat{\theta}_{A^{\prime},B^{\prime}}(\alpha\otimes\beta)(f(a)\otimes g(b))\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{|f||g|+|g||a|}\alpha[f(a)\beta(g(b))].\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Now make
\Delta_{2}:=[\hat{\theta}_{A,B}(\hat{D}(f)\otimes\hat{D}(g))(\alpha\otimes\beta)](a\otimes b)
, then we get

\begin{matrix}\Delta_{2}&=&[\hat{\theta}_{A,B}(\hat{D}(f)\otimes\hat{D}(g))(\alpha\otimes\beta)](a\otimes b)\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{|D(g)||\alpha|}\hat{\theta}_{A,B}(\hat{D}(f)(\alpha)\otimes\hat{D}(g)(\beta))(a\otimes b)\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{|g||\alpha|}\hat{\theta}_{A,B}(\alpha f\otimes\beta g)(a\otimes b)\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{|g||\alpha|}\alpha f(a\beta g(b))=(-1)^{|g||\alpha|}\alpha[f(a\beta(g(b))].\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

If
-|\alpha|\not=|f(a)|
, since

\alpha\in{\rm Hom}_{S}^{|\alpha|}(A^{\prime},S)={\rm Hom}_{S}(A^{\prime}_{-|\alpha|},S)
, we obtain that
\alpha[f(a\beta(g(b))]=0
and,
hence,
\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{2}
. Otherwise, we have
-|\alpha|=|f|+|a|
and hence

(-1)^{|f||g|+|g||a|}=(-1)^{|g||\alpha|}.

∎

Definition 2.8
.

Given
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
, the isomorphism

\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{n})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}\ \ }}\hat{D}(A_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n})

is defined recursively, for
n\geq 3
, as the composition
of

\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{n})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ id_{\hat{D}(A_{1})}\otimes\hat{\theta}_{A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}}\ \ }}\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{2}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n})

with

\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{2}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \hat{\theta}_{A_{1},A_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{n}}\ \ }}\hat{D}(A_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n}).

Moreover, for any
A\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
, we make
\hat{\theta}_{A}:=id_{\hat{D}(A)}
, by definition.

Then, given
n\geq 1
and a typical homogeneous generator
\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n}
of the graded tensor product
\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{n})
and a typical homogeneous generator
a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}
of the graded tensor product
A_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n}
, we have

\hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\alpha_{1}(a_{1}\alpha_{2}(a_{2}(\alpha_{3}\cdots a_{n-1}\alpha_{n}(a_{n}))\cdots).

The inverse

\hat{\tau}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}:\hat{D}(A_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{n})

of
\hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}
will be relevant in the following.

Lemma 2.9
.

Given
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
and an increasing sequence natural numbers
0=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{\ell-1}<i_{\ell}=n
,
\ell\geq 2
, we have the following associativity property for the isomorphisms
\hat{\theta}
.
Make
I_{j}:=(A_{i_{j-1}+1},A_{i_{j-1}+2},\ldots,A_{i_{j}})
and

A^{\otimes I_{j}}:=A_{i_{j-1}+1}\otimes A_{i_{j-1}+2}\otimes\cdots\otimes A_{i_{j}}
, for
j\in[1,\ell]
.
Then, we have

\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},\ldots,A^{\otimes I_{\ell}}}(\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{\ell}})=\hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}.

Taking inverses the formula becomes

(\hat{\tau}_{I_{1}}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{I_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{\tau}_{I_{\ell}})\hat{\tau}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},\ldots,A^{\otimes I_{\ell}}}=\hat{\tau}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}.

Proof.

For
\ell=2
, if
\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n}\in\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{D}(A_{n})
is a typical homogeneous generator, we have

\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},A^{\otimes I_{2}}}(\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}})(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})=\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},A^{\otimes I_{2}}}(\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{1}})\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}(\alpha_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})).

Evaluating at a typical homogeneous generator
a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}
we get

\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{1}})[a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i_{1}}\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}(\alpha_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})(a_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})]

which clearly coincides with
\hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})
.
So we can proceed by induction. Assume that the lemma holds for sequences
with length
\ell-1
and let us show it for a sequence of length
\ell
.

We have that

\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},\ldots,A^{\otimes I_{\ell}}}(\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{\ell}})(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})

equals

\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{1}},\ldots,A^{\otimes I_{\ell}}}[\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{1}})\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}(\alpha_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{2}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{\ell}}(\alpha_{i_{\ell-1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})].

Evaluating at a typical homogeneous generator
a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}
, we obtain

\hat{\theta}_{I_{1}}(\alpha_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{i_{1}})[a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i_{1}}H]

where

H=\hat{\theta}_{A^{\otimes I_{2}},\ldots,A^{\otimes I_{\ell}}}(\hat{\theta}_{I_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{\theta}_{I_{\ell}})(\alpha_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})(a_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})
. By induction hypothesis, we have

H=\hat{\theta}_{A_{i_{1}+1},\ldots,A_{n}}(\alpha_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\alpha_{n})(a_{i_{1}+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}).

Then, we have the wanted formula.
∎

Remark 2.10
.

For any natural number
n\geq 2
, and
d_{1},\ldots,d_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we can define

\lambda_{n}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}d_{i}d_{j}.

So, we have
\lambda_{n}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{n})=\lambda_{n-1}(d_{2},\ldots,d_{n})+d_{1}(d_{2}+\cdots+d_{n})
. Then, from (
2.7
), we obtain the following lemma by induction on the natural number
n
.

Lemma 2.11
.

Given homogeneous morphisms
A_{1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{1}}}B_{1},\ldots,A_{n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{n}}}B_{n}
in
{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
we have a commutative diagram

\begin{matrix}\hat{D}(B_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(B_{n})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \hat{\theta}_{B_{1},\ldots,B_{n}}\ \ }}&\hat{D}(B_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}B_{n})\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hat{D}(f_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{D}(f_{n})$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle(-1)^{\lambda_{n}(|f_{1}|,\ldots,|f_{n}|)}\hat{D}(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{n})$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow\\
\hat{D}(A_{1})\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}\hat{D}(A_{n})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \hat{\theta}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}\ \ }}&\hat{D}(A_{1}\otimes_{S}\cdots\otimes_{S}A_{n}).\\
\end{matrix}

Taking inverses, we obtain the formula:

(\hat{D}(f_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\hat{D}(f_{n}))\hat{\tau}_{B_{1},\ldots,B_{n}}=(-1)^{\lambda_{n}(|f_{1}|,\ldots,|f_{n}|)}\hat{\tau}_{A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}}\hat{D}(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{n}).

Definition 2.12
.

Once we fix some
B\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
, we can consider the following sequence of
canonical isomorphisms:

1.

For
n=1
, we make
\hat{\theta}_{1}:=id_{\hat{D}(B)}:\hat{D}(B)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(B)
; and,
for
n\geq 2
, we make
\hat{\theta}_{n}:=\hat{\theta}_{B,\ldots,B}:\hat{D}(B)^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(B^{\otimes n})
.

and their inverses:

2.

For
n=1
, we make
\hat{\tau}_{1}:=id_{\hat{D}(B)}:\hat{D}(B)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(B)
; and,
for
n\geq 2
, we make
\hat{\tau}_{n}:=\hat{\tau}_{B,\ldots,B}:\hat{D}(B^{\otimes n})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\hat{D}(B)^{\otimes n}
.

Lemma 2.13
.

Given
B\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
, non-negative integers
r,s,t,n
with
r+s+t=n
and
s\geq 1
, and any homogeneous morphism of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules
b_{s}:B^{\otimes s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
of degree
|b_{s}|=1
, we have
the following equality of morphisms from
\hat{D}(B^{\otimes(r+1+t)})
to
\hat{D}(B)^{\otimes n}

(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{s}\hat{D}(b_{s})\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})\hat{\tau}_{r+1+t}=\hat{\tau}_{n}\hat{D}(id_{B}^{\otimes r}\otimes b_{s}\otimes id_{B}^{\otimes t}).

Proof.

Make
A_{i}=B
, for all
i\in[1,n]
; let
\ell:=r+1+t
and consider the sequence
0=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{\ell}=n
defined by
i_{j}:=j
, for
j\in[1,r]
;
i_{r+1}:=r+s
; and
i_{j}:=j
, for
j\in[r+2,\ell]
. Then, from the associativity formula in
(
2.9
), we have

(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{s}\otimes id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes t})\hat{\tau}_{J_{r},B^{\otimes s},J_{t}}=\hat{\tau}_{n},

where, for
m\geq 0
, we denote by
J_{m}:=(B,B,\ldots,B)
, a sequence with
m
terms.

Now, apply the formula in (
2.11
) to the family
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r+1+t}
defined by
f_{i}:=id_{B}
, for
i\in[1,r]\cup[r+2,r+1+t]
, and
f_{r+1}:=b_{s}:B^{\otimes s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
.
Thus
|f_{r+1}|=1

and
|f_{i}|=0
for
i\not=r+1
. Hence
\lambda_{r+1+t}(|f_{1}|,\ldots,|f_{r+1+t}|)=0
, and we get
the formula

(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{D}(b_{s})\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})\hat{\tau}_{r+1+t}=\hat{\tau}_{J_{r},B^{\otimes s},J_{t}}\hat{D}(id_{B}^{\otimes r}\otimes b_{s}\otimes id_{B}^{\otimes t}).

Then, if
\Delta=(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{s}\hat{D}(b_{s})\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})\hat{\tau}_{r+1+t}
,
we have

\begin{matrix}\Delta&=&(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{D}(b_{s})\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})\hat{\tau}_{r+1+t}\hfill\\
&=&(id_{\hat{D}(B)}^{\otimes r}\otimes\hat{\tau}_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t}_{\hat{D}(B)})\hat{\tau}_{J_{r},B^{\otimes s},J_{t}}\hat{D}(id_{B}^{\otimes r}\otimes b_{s}\otimes id_{B}^{\otimes t})\hfill\\
&=&\hat{\tau}_{n}\hat{D}(id_{B}^{\otimes r}\otimes b_{s}\otimes id_{B}^{\otimes t}).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

∎

3
The Yoneda
A_{\infty}
-algebra

Assume that
S
is a finite product of copies of the field
k

and
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit element of
S
as a sum of orthogonal idempotents.
We recall the following basic definition.

Definition 3.1
.

An
A_{\infty}
-algebra

A
is a graded
S
-
S
-bimodule

A
, equipped with a sequence of homogeneous
morphisms of
S
-
S
-bimodules

\{m_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}},

where each
m_{n}
has degree
|m_{n}|=2-n
, such that, for each

n\in\mathbb{N}
, the following
Stasheff identity
holds.

\begin{matrix}S_{n}:=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}r+s+t=n\\
s\geq 1;r,t\geq 0\end{matrix}}(-1)^{r+st}m_{r+1+t}(id^{\otimes r}\otimes m_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t})=0.\end{matrix}

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\widehat{A}
be an
augmented graded
S
-algebra
, that is the
S
-
S
-bimodule
\widehat{A}
admits a decomposition of graded
S
-algebras
\widehat{A}=S\oplus A
and has
1_{S}
as unit element; thus the product of
\widehat{A}
restricts to a product on the graded
S
-algebra
A
. Then, whenever
A
admits a structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra

\{m_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{1}=0
and
m_{2}
is induced by the product of
A
, we have that
\widehat{A}
admits a unique structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra

\{\widehat{m}_{n}:\widehat{A}^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\widehat{A}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\widehat{m}_{1}=0
and
\widehat{m}_{2}
is induced by the product of
\widehat{A}
and, moreover:
\widehat{m}_{n}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, for all
n\geq 3
and
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}\in\widehat{A}
with
z_{u}\in\{e_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
, for some
u\in[1,n]
.

Proof.

Make
\widehat{m}_{1}=0
and let
\widehat{m}_{2}:\widehat{A}\otimes_{S}\widehat{A}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\widehat{A}
be the morphism of
S
-
S
-bimodules induced by the product of
\widehat{A}
. For
n\geq 3
, we have an
S
-
S
-bimodule decomposition
\widehat{A}^{\otimes n}=A^{\otimes n}\oplus E_{n}
, where
E_{n}
denotes the
S
-
S
-subbimodule generated by tensors with some factor in
\{e_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
. Then, consider the extension
\widehat{m}_{n}:\widehat{A}^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\widehat{A}
of
m_{n}
which maps
E_{n}
to zero. Thus
|m_{n}|=|\widehat{m}_{n}|
for all
n
.

We claim that

\widehat{S}_{n}=0
, for all
n\geq 1
, where

\widehat{S}_{n}=\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}r+s+t=n\\
s\geq 1;r,t\geq 0\end{matrix}}(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(id^{\otimes r}\otimes\widehat{m}_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t}).

Since
\widehat{m}_{1}=0
, we clearly have that
\widehat{S}_{1}=\widehat{m}_{1}^{2}=0
and

\widehat{S}_{2}=-\widehat{m}_{2}(\widehat{m}_{1}\otimes id)-\widehat{m}_{2}(id\otimes\widehat{m}_{1})+\widehat{m}_{1}\widehat{m_{2}}=0.

For
n\geq 3
, we consider a typical non-zero generator

z=z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n}\in\widehat{A}^{\otimes n}
, where we assume that all the elements

z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}\in\widehat{A}
are directed, see (
2.2
).
Let us examine
\widehat{S}_{n}(z)
. If all
z_{i}\in A
, we have
\widehat{S}_{n}(z)=S_{n}(z)=0
. So, assume that
z_{u}\in\{e_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
, for some
u\in[1,n]
.
Consider the index set
I=\{(r,s,t)\mid r+s+t=n;s\geq 1;r,t\geq 0\}
. Then,
\widehat{S}_{n}=\sum_{(r,s,t)\in I}\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)
, where

\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t):=(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(id^{\otimes r}\otimes\widehat{m}_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t}).

We prove that
\widehat{S}_{n}(z)=0
in each one of the following three possible cases.

Case 1:

u=1
.

In each one of the following situations, we have that
\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)=0
.

1.1

If we have
r>1
;

1.2

If we have
r=1
and
t\not=0
;

1.3

If we have
r=0
and (
s=1
or
s\geq 3)
;

There are only two other possible situations:

1.4

If we have
r=0
and
s=2
. Thus,
t=n-2
and

\begin{matrix}\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)&=&(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(\widehat{m}_{2}(e_{i}\otimes z_{2})\otimes z_{3}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})\hfill\\
&=&\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{2}\otimes z_{3}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n}).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

1.5

If we have
r=1
and
t=0
. Thus,
s=n-1
and

\begin{matrix}\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)&=&(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(e_{i}\otimes\widehat{m}_{s}(z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n}))\hfill\\
&=&-\widehat{m}_{2}(e_{i}\otimes\widehat{m}_{s}(z_{2}\otimes z_{3}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n}))\hfill\\
&=&-\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{2}\otimes z_{3}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Therefore, we get
\widehat{S}_{n}(z)=\widehat{S}_{n}(0,2,n-2)(z)+\widehat{S}_{n}(1,n-1,0)(z)=0
.

Case 2:

u=n
.

This case is similar to the preceding one. Indeed, in each one of the following situations, we have that
\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)=0
:

2.1

If we have
t>1
;

2.2

If we have
t=1
and
r\not=0
;

2.3

If we have
t=0
and (
s=1
or
s\geq 3)
;

There are only two other possible situations:

2.4

If we have
t=0
and
s=2
. Thus,
r=n-2
and

\begin{matrix}\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)&=&(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{r}\otimes\widehat{m}_{2}(z_{n-1}\otimes e_{i}))\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{n-2}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n-1}).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

2.5

If we have
t=1
and
r=0
. Thus,
s=n-1
and

\begin{matrix}\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)&=&(-1)^{r+st}\widehat{m}_{r+1+t}(\widehat{m}_{s}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n-1})\otimes e_{i})\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{n-1}\widehat{m}_{2}(\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n-1})\otimes e_{i})\hfill\\
&=&(-1)^{n-1}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n-1}).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Therefore, we get
\widehat{S}_{n}(z)=\widehat{S}_{n}(n-2,2,0)(z)+\widehat{S}_{n}(0,n-1,1)(z)=0
.

Case 3:

1<u<n
.

Again, in each one of the following situations, we have that
\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)=0
:

3.1

If we have
r\geq u
;

3.2

If we have
t\geq n-u+1
(so
u\in[n-t+1,n-1]
);

3.3

If we have
r<u
and
t<n-u+1
, and (
s=1
or
s\geq 3)
;

If we are not in these situations, we have
r<u
,
t<n-u+1
and
s=2
. Thus, we are left with two cases:

3.4

We have
r=u-1
, and
s=2
, thus
t=n-u-1
; or

3.5

We have
r=u-2
, and
s=2
, thus
t=n-u
.

In case 3.4, we have that
\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)=\widehat{S}_{n}(u-1,2,n-u-1)(z)
equals

(-1)^{u-1}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{u-1}\otimes\widehat{m}_{2}(e_{i}\otimes z_{u+1})\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})

that is

(-1)^{u-1}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{u-1}\otimes z_{u+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})
.

In case 3.5, we have that
\widehat{S}_{n}(r,s,t)(z)=\widehat{S}_{n}(u-2,2,n-u)(z)
equals

(-1)^{u-2}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{r}\otimes\widehat{m}_{2}(z_{u-1}\otimes e_{i})\otimes z_{n-t+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})

that is

(-1)^{u-2}\widehat{m}_{n-1}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{u-1}\otimes z_{u+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})
.
Therefore, we get

\widehat{S}_{n}(z)=\widehat{S}_{n}(u-1,2,n-u-1)(z)+\widehat{S}_{n}(u-2,2,n-u)(z)=0.

∎

Definition 3.3
.

Assume that
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a family of non-isomorphic indecomposable
\Lambda
-modules, where
\Lambda
is a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra. Make
\Delta=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\Delta_{i}
. Then,
since
k
is algebraically closed, there is a splitting of the endomorphism algebra
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta)=S\bigoplus J
over its radical
J
. Moreover, the semisimple subalgebra
S
of
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta)
can be described as
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, where
e_{i}=\sigma_{i}\pi_{i}\in{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta)
is the idempotent defined by the composition of the canonical projection
\pi_{i}:\Delta\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta_{i}
and the canonical injection
\sigma_{i}:\Delta_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta
.

The Yoneda algebra of
\Delta
is the graded
S
-algebra

A=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}A_{n},\hbox{ where }A_{n}={\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{n}(\Delta,\Delta),\hbox{ for }n\geq 0.

Definition 3.4
.

Assume, as before, that
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a family of non-isomorphic indecomposable
\Lambda
-modules, where
\Lambda
is a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra. Make
\Delta=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\Delta_{i}
.
Let
P_{\Delta_{i}}
be a fixed projective resolution of
\Delta_{i}
, for each
i\in{\cal P}
, and consider them as graded differential
\Lambda
-modules, where
\Lambda
is considered as a differential graded
k
-algebra concentrated at degree 0. Make
P_{\Delta}:=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P_{\Delta_{i}}
and

{\cal E}={\cal E}(P_{\Delta}):=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm End}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta}),

where
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}^{n}(P_{\Delta})
is the space of homogeneous morphisms
f:P_{\Delta}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta}
of graded
\Lambda
-modules with degree
|f|=n
; it can be identified with
{\rm Hom}^{0}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta}[n])
.

The
k
-algebra
{\cal E}
admits a differential
d:{\cal E}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal E}
given on homogeneous elements
f\in{\cal E}
by the formula
d(f)=d_{P_{\Delta}}f-(-1)^{|f|}fd_{P_{\Delta}}
. So we have a differential graded
k
-algebra
{\cal E}
.

For each
i\in{\cal P}
, denote by
\pi^{*}_{i}:P_{\Delta}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{i}}
and

\sigma^{*}_{i}:P_{\Delta_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta}
the projection and the injection, respectively, associated to the direct sum
P_{\Delta}=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P_{\Delta_{i}}
, and make
e^{*}_{i}:=\sigma^{*}_{i}\pi_{i}^{*}
. Then, we obtain a decomposition
id_{P_{\Delta}}=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}^{*}
of the unit of the algebra
{\cal E}
as a sum of orthogonal idempotents.

There is a canonical isomorphism from
S
onto

\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}^{*}
, and since
d(e_{i}^{*})=0
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
, the
differential graded endomorphism algebra
{\cal E}
of
P_{\Delta}
is in fact a differential graded
S
-algebra.

Remark 3.5
.

For any family
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
as above,
a well known result of homological algebra states that there is an isomorphism of graded
k
-algebras

\Phi:H({\cal E}(P_{\Delta}))\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{n}(\Delta,\Delta),

where we take
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta):=0
, for
n<0
.

Moreover, it is easy to see that
\Phi(\overline{e_{i}^{*}})=e_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
, where
\overline{e_{i}^{*}}
denotes the class of the
0
-cycle
e_{i}^{*}
in the homology graded
S
-algebra
H({\cal E}(P_{\Delta}))
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Assume that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is a strict homological system for the
k
-algebra
\Lambda
.
Consider a fixed
i
-bounded projective resolution
P_{\Delta_{i}}
of
\Delta_{i}
, for each
i\in{\cal P}
, as in (
1.6
), and adopt the notation of the preceding definition (
3.4
). The following holds.

1.

We have a
d
-invariant decomposition
{\cal E}={\cal E}^{\prime}\oplus{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}
of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules,
where

{\cal E}^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}e_{j}^{*}{\cal E}e_{i}^{*}\hbox{ \ and \ }{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}=\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\not\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}e_{j}^{*}{\cal E}e_{i}^{*}.

Moreover,
{\cal E}^{\prime}
is a differential graded
S
-subalgebra of
{\cal E}
and
H({\cal E}^{\prime\prime})=0
.

2.

We have a
d
-invariant decomposition

{\cal E}^{\prime}=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}^{*}]\oplus{\cal R}^{\prime}
of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules,
where
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is a differential graded
S
-subalgebra of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
. Therefore,

H({\cal E})=H({\cal E}^{\prime})=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}k\overline{e_{i}^{*}}]\oplus H({\cal R}^{\prime}).

Proof.

(1): We will use the graded isomorphism
\Phi
of (
3.5
). Clearly, there is a decomposition as in (1) of graded vector spaces.
Since
e_{j}{\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{n}(\Delta,\Delta)e_{i}={\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{n}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})=0
, for all
n\geq 0
and all
\overline{i}\not\leq\overline{j}
, the restriction of the isomorphism
\Phi
gives

H({\cal E}^{\prime\prime})\cong\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\not\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}e_{j}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta)e_{i}=0.

Moreover, the spaces
{\cal E}^{\prime}
and
{\cal E}^{\prime\prime}
are invariant under the differential, because
d(e_{i}^{*})=0
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

(2)
In order to describe the ideal
{\cal R}^{\prime}
of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
,
observe that we have the following isomorphisms for
i,j\in{\cal P}
:

\phi_{i,j}^{n}:e^{*}_{j}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta})e^{*}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{j}}),\hbox{ given by }f\mapsto f_{i,j}:=\pi_{j}^{*}f\sigma^{*}_{i},

and

\psi_{i,j}:\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{j}})\cong\bigoplus_{s,n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{s+n}_{\Delta_{j}})=\bigoplus_{s,t\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}})

given, for
f_{i,j}\in{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{j}})
, by
\psi_{i,j}(f_{i,j})=\sum_{s}f_{i,j,s,s+n}
,
where
f_{i,j,s,s+n}:P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{s+n}_{\Delta_{j}}
is the restriction of
f_{i,j}
. Then, we obtain the following
sequence of isomorphisms

\begin{matrix}{\cal E}^{\prime}&=&\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}e_{j}^{*}[\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta})]e_{i}^{*}\hfill\\
&=&\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e_{j}^{*}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta})e_{i}^{*}\hfill\\
&\cong&\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{j}}),\hfill\\
&\cong&\bigoplus_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}i,j\in{\cal P}\\
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}\\
\end{matrix}}\bigoplus_{s,t\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}})=:\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Denote by
\phi=\bigoplus_{i,j,n}\phi^{n}_{i,j}
the first isomorphism and by
\psi=\bigoplus_{i,j}\psi_{i,j}
the second one. Then, we have that

\phi(e_{i}^{*})=id_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}\in{\rm Hom}^{0}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{i}})
, thus we have

\psi\phi(e_{i}^{*})=\sum_{s}id_{P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}}\in\bigoplus_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}})
.

The space
\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
has a natural structure of
k
-algebra, given typical generators

P_{\Delta_{i}}^{s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ f_{i,u,s,r}\ }}P_{\Delta_{u}}^{r}\hbox{ and }P^{l}_{\Delta_{v}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ g_{v,j,l,t}\ }}P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}},

the product
g_{v,j,l,t}\cdot f_{i,u,s,r}
is
g_{u,j,r,t}\circ f_{i,u,s,r}
if
v=u
and
l=r
, and it is zero otherwise.
The morphism
\psi\phi:{\cal E}^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
is in fact an isomorphism of
k
-algebras. Indeed,
given
f\in e^{*}_{t}{\rm Hom}^{n}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta})e^{*}_{i}

and
g\in e^{*}_{j}{\rm Hom}^{m}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta},P_{\Delta})e^{*}_{t}
, we have

(gf)_{i,j}=\sum_{t^{\prime}}g_{t^{\prime},j}f_{i,t^{\prime}}=g_{t,j}f_{i,t}\in{\rm Hom}^{n+m}_{\Lambda}(P_{\Delta_{i}},P_{\Delta_{j}})
, so

\begin{matrix}\psi\phi(gf)=\psi((gf)_{i,j})&=&\psi(g_{t,j}f_{i,t})\hfill\\
&=&\sum_{s}(g_{t,j}f_{i,t})_{s,s+n+m}\hfill\\
&=&\sum_{s}g_{t,j,s+n,s+n+m}f_{i,t,s,s+n}.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Therefore,

\begin{matrix}\psi\phi(g)\psi\phi(f)&=&\psi(g_{t,j})\psi(f_{i,t})\hfill\\
&=&(\sum_{r}g_{t,j,r,r+m})(\sum_{s}f_{i,t,s,s+n})\hfill\\
&=&\sum_{s}g_{t,j,s+n,s+n+m}f_{i,t,s,s+n}=\psi\phi(gf).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Since
k
is algebraically closed and
P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}\cong P_{i}
, its endomorphism algebra splits over its radical, so we obtain

{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}})=kid_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}\oplus{\rm rad}_{\Lambda}(P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}),

where
{\rm rad}_{\Lambda}(P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}})
consists of the non-isomorphisms of

{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}})
.

Now consider the linear subspace
{\cal R}^{\prime}=(\psi\phi)^{-1}(\underline{\cal R}^{\prime})
of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
, where

\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
is the linear subspace of
\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
generated by the morphisms of
\Lambda
-modules

f:P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}}
such that
s\not=0
or
t\not=0
, or, if
s=0=t
, then
f
is not an isomorphism.

In order to show that
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is an ideal of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
, take any typical linear generator
g:P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}}
of
\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
and any typical generator
f:P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{u}}^{r}
of
\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
with possible non-zero product
fg:P_{\Delta_{i}}^{s}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P_{\Delta_{u}}^{r}
. If
s\not=0
or
r\not=0
, we get
fg\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
. So assume that
s=0=r
and that
fg
is an isomorphism. Then,
P_{i}=P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}
is a direct summand of

P^{t}_{\Delta_{j}}
. Since
P_{\Delta_{j}}
is a
j
-bounded minimal projective resolution, if
t<0
, we get that

\overline{i}>\overline{j}
, which is not the case because
g\in\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
implies that
\overline{i}\leq\overline{j}
. Thus
t=0
, so

P_{i}\cong P_{j}
, and we obtain
i=j
. Then, the composition
fg
belongs to the radical

{\rm rad}_{\Lambda}(P_{i},P_{i})
, because
g
does, a contradiction.
Hence
fg
is not an isomorphism and, so,
fg\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
.
This shows that
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is a left ideal of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
. Similarly, one shows that
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is a right ideal of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.

Let us show that
{\cal E}^{\prime}=[\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}ke^{*}_{i}]+{\cal R}^{\prime}
.
Any
f=\sum_{i,j,s,t}f_{i,j,s,t}\in\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
, can be written
as follows:

f=\sum_{s\not=0\hbox{ \scriptsize{or} }t\not=0}f_{i,j,s,t}+\sum_{i\not=j}f_{i,j,0,0}+\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}id_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}+\sum_{i}\rho_{i}^{f},

where
f_{i,i,0,0}=c^{f}_{i}id_{P_{\Delta_{i}}^{0}}+\rho_{i}^{f}\in kid_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}\oplus{\rm rad}_{\Lambda}(P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}},P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}})
. Moreover, we have

\psi\phi(e_{i}^{*})=\sum_{s}id_{P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}}=id_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}-\rho_{i},\hbox{ \ where \ }\rho_{i}=\sum_{s<0}id_{P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}}.

Then, in the preceding expression for
f
, we can replace

\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}id_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}\hbox{ \ \ by \ \ }\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}\psi\phi(e^{*}_{i})+\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}\rho_{i},

to obtain that
f=\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}\psi\phi(e^{*}_{i})+\sum_{i}c_{i}^{f}\widehat{\rho}_{i}
, for some
\widehat{\rho}_{i}\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
. Therefore, we obtain
{\cal E}^{\prime}=[\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}ke^{*}_{i}]+{\cal R}^{\prime}
.

If
\sum_{i}c_{i}e_{i}^{*}=0
, we get
\psi\phi(\sum_{i}c_{i}e_{i}^{*})=\sum_{i}\sum_{s}c_{i}id_{P^{s}_{\Delta_{i}}}
, a combination of linearly independent elements in
\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
, thus all the coefficient are zero and

\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}ke^{*}_{i}=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke^{*}_{i}
.
Moreover, it is clear that
[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}kid_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}]\cap\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}=0
, thus if
\sum_{i}c_{i}e_{i}^{*}\in{\cal R}^{\prime}
, we obtain that

\sum_{i}c_{i}id_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}-\sum_{i}c_{i}\rho_{i}=\psi\phi(\sum_{i}c_{i}e_{i}^{*})\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
, thus
\sum_{i}c_{i}id_{P^{0}_{\Delta_{i}}}\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
and all the coefficients are zero, so we finally get
{\cal E}^{\prime}=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke^{*}_{i}]\oplus{\cal R}^{\prime}
as we wanted.

We claim that
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is a
d
-invariant subalgebra of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
. Notice first that
\phi(d_{P_{\Delta}})=\sum_{i}d_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}
, thus

\psi\phi(d_{P_{\Delta}})=\sum_{i}\psi(d_{P_{\Delta_{i}}})=\sum_{i,s}d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}
. Now, take any typical generator
f=f_{i,j,r,s}\in\underline{\cal E}^{\prime}
, so
f^{\prime}=(\psi\phi)^{-1}(f)
is a typical generator of
{\cal E}^{\prime}
, which has degree
n:=s-r
. Then we have

\begin{matrix}\psi\phi(d(f^{\prime}))&=&\psi\phi(d_{P_{\Delta}}f^{\prime}-(-1)^{n}f^{\prime}d_{P_{\Delta}})\hfill\\
&=&\psi\phi(d_{P_{\Delta}})f-(-1)^{n}f\psi\phi(d_{P_{\Delta}})\hfill\\
&=&\sum_{u,a}d^{a}_{P_{\Delta_{u}}}f-(-1)^{n}\sum_{v,b}fd^{b}_{P_{\Delta_{v}}}\hfill\\
&=&d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}f-(-1)^{n}fd^{r-1}_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

These maps are disposed as follows

\begin{matrix}P_{\Delta_{i}}^{r-1}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ d^{r-1}_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}\ }}&P^{r}_{\Delta_{i}}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ f\ }}&P_{\Delta_{j}}^{s}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}\ }}&P^{s+1}_{\Delta_{j}}.\end{matrix}

Let us examine
d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}f
. If
r\not=0
or
s+1\not=0
, then
d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}f\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
. If
r=0
and
s=-1
, and
d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}f
is an isomorphism, we get that
P_{i}
is a direct summand of
P^{-1}_{\Delta_{j}}
and, since
P_{\Delta_{j}}
is a
j
-bounded
projective resolution, we obtain
\overline{i}>\overline{j}
, which is not the case. Thus
d^{s}_{P_{\Delta_{j}}}f
is not an isomorphism and belongs to
\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
. Similarly, one shows that
fd^{r-1}_{P_{\Delta_{i}}}\in\underline{\cal R}^{\prime}
. Therefore, we get
d(f^{\prime})\in{\cal R}^{\prime}
. So, we finally obtain that
d({\cal R}^{\prime})\subseteq{\cal R}^{\prime}
.

Then, we have a
d
-invariant decomposition
{\cal E}^{\prime}=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}^{*}]\oplus{\cal R}^{\prime}
, where
{\cal R^{\prime}}
is a differential graded
k
-algebra and then
Z({\cal E}^{\prime})=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}^{*}]\oplus Z({\cal R}^{\prime})
and
B({\cal E}^{\prime})=B({\cal R^{\prime}})
, thus

H({\cal E})=H({\cal E}^{\prime})=[\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}k\overline{e_{i}^{*}}]\oplus H({\cal R}^{\prime}).

Notice that if we identify
S
with
\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}^{*}
, we obtain that
{\cal R}^{\prime}
is differential graded (without unit)
S
-algebra.
∎

The following theorem generalizes the argument of
[
8
]
(3.5), which relies on a theorem by Kadeishvili, see
[
8
]
(3.3),
[
7
]
and
[
14
]
.

Theorem 3.7
.

Assume that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is a strict homological system for the
k
-algebra
\Lambda
. Then, the Yoneda graded
S
-algebra
A

of the
\Lambda
-module
\Delta=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\Delta_{i}
admits a structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra with
S
-
S
-bimodule higher multiplications
\{m_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
m_{1}=0
,
m_{2}
is induced by the product of the Yoneda algebra and, if
n\geq 3
, they satisfy that
m_{n}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, whenever
z_{i}\in\{e_{j}\mid j\in{\cal P}\}
, for some
i\in[1,n]
.

Proof.

Adopt the notation of the preceding Proposition. Apply Kadeishvili theorem to the differential graded
S
-algebra
{\cal R}^{\prime}
, to obtain a structure
\{m_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
A_{\infty}
-algebra on the graded homology
S
-algebra
H({\cal R}^{\prime})
, such that
m_{1}=0
and
m_{2}
is induced by the product of
H({\cal R}^{\prime})
.

Then, apply (
3.2
) to the augmented graded
S
-algebra

H({\cal E}^{\prime})=[\oplus_{i\in{\cal P}}k\overline{e_{i}^{*}}]\oplus H({\cal R}^{\prime})
, to obtain a structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra
\{\widehat{m}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
on
H({\cal E}^{\prime})
such that
\widehat{m}_{1}=0
,
\widehat{m}_{2}
is induced by the product of
H({\cal E}^{\prime})
, and
\widehat{m}_{n}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, for
n\geq 3
and
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}\in H({\cal E}^{\prime})
, whenever

z_{i}\in\{\overline{e_{j}^{*}}\mid j\in{\cal P}\}
, for some
i\in[1,n]
.

Finally, we can derive the statement of the theorem by transfering the structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra of
H({\cal E})=H({\cal E}^{\prime})
onto the Yoneda
algebra
A
with the help of the graded isomorphism
\Phi:H({\cal E})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A
mentioned in (
3.5
).
∎

Remark 3.8
.

We can consider the Yoneda algebra
A
associated to any admissible homological system
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
for a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
. Assume that
A
admits a structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra with higher multiplications
\{m_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Notice that, for
n\geq 3
, the following are equivalent:

1.

m_{n}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, whenever
z_{i}\in\{e_{j}\mid j\in{\cal P}\}
, for some
i\in[1,n]
;

2.

m_{n}(z_{1}\otimes z_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, whenever
z_{i}=id_{\Delta}
, for some
i\in[1,n]
.

We will say that the
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A

has a strict unit
or that it
has a strict structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra
(or even that
A
is
strict
) if
m_{1}=0
,
m_{2}
is induced by the product of the Yoneda algebra, and the preceding property of the higher multiplications of
A
is satisfied.

4
Algebras linked to strict Yoneda
A_{\infty}
-algebras

From now on, unless otherwise specified, we will keep the notation of the following remarks.

Remark 4.1
.

We denote by
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})

a fixed admissible homological system for a given finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Lambda
, as in (
1.3
). Consider the
\Lambda
-module
\Delta=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\Delta_{i}
. Then, we have a decomposition
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta)=S\bigoplus J
, where
J
is the Jacobson radical of
{\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta)
and
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, where
\{e_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
are the canonical central idempotents of
S
considered in (
3.3
).

For the study of the category
{\cal F}(\Delta)
, the Yoneda algebra

A=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}A_{n},\hbox{ \ where \ }A_{n}={\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{n}(\Delta,\Delta),\hbox{ for }n\geq 0,

of
\Delta
is an important tool.
From now on, we assume that the Yoneda algebra
A

admits a strict structure
\{m_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
A_{\infty}
-algebra as in (
3.8
), thus
m_{1}=0
,
m_{2}
is induced by the product of
A
, and, for
n\geq 3
, we have
m_{n}(z_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes z_{n})=0
, whenever
z_{u}\in\{e_{i}\mid i\in{\cal P}\}
for some
u\in[1,n]
.

From (
3.7
), we know that this is the case whenever
{\cal H}
is a strict homological system. We will prove in (
12.8
) that this is the only possible case.

Definition 4.2
.

We refer to the preceding
A_{\infty}
-algebra
A
as
the Yoneda
A_{\infty}
-algebra of
\Delta
.
Now, we consider the
bar construction
B
of this
A_{\infty}
-algebra. That is we consider the shifted graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
B:=A[1]
, endowed with the sequence
\{b_{n}:B^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of
S
-
S
-bimodule morphisms given by the following commutative squares

\begin{matrix}A^{\otimes n}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\sigma^{\otimes n}}}&B^{\otimes n}\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle m_{n}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle b_{n}$}}$\hss}\\
A&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\sigma}}&B,\\
\end{matrix}

where
\sigma_{n}:A\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}A[1]=B
is the identity map, considered as a homogeneous morphism of graded
S
-
S
-bimodules of degree
-1
. Thus, each
b_{n}:B^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B
is a homogeneous morphism of degree
|b_{n}|=1
, and
b_{1}=0
. Then, the
family
\{b_{n}:B^{\otimes n}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}B\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

satisfies the basic relations

\sum_{\scriptsize\begin{matrix}r+s+t=n\\
r,t\geq 0;s\geq 1\end{matrix}}b_{r+1+t}(id^{\otimes r}\otimes b_{s}\otimes id^{\otimes t})=0,\hbox{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

Remark 4.3
.

Adopt the preceding notation.

1.

The homogeneous summands of the graded
S
-
S
-bimodule
B\in{\rm G\underline{b}}\hbox{-}S
are

\begin{matrix}B_{j}=A_{j+1}=0,\hbox{ for }j<-1;\hfill\\
B_{-1}=A_{0}={\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta);\hfill\\
B_{0}=A_{1}={\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta);\hfill\\
B_{j}=A_{j+1}={\rm Ext}^{j+1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta),\hbox{ for }j\geq 1.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

2.

For the graded dual
S
-
S
-bimodule
\hat{D}(B)\in{\rm G\overline{b}}\hbox{-}S
we have:

\begin{matrix}\hat{D}(B)_{j}=\hat{D}(B_{-j})=0,\hbox{ for }j>1;\hfill\\
\hat{D}(B)_{1}=\hat{D}(B_{-1})=\hat{D}({\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\Delta));\hfill\\
\hat{D}(B)_{0}=\hat{D}(B_{0})=\hat{D}({\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta));\hfill\\
\hat{D}(B)_{-j}=\hat{D}(B_{j})=\hat{D}({\rm Ext}^{j+1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta)),\hbox{ for }j>0.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

3.

For
i,j\in{\cal P}
, we have

e_{j}B_{-1}e_{i}=e_{j}{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta)e_{i}\cong{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})
, hence

e_{j}*\hat{D}(B)_{1}*e_{i}=e_{i}D(B)_{1}e_{j}=e_{i}D(B_{-1})e_{j}\cong D(e_{j}B_{-1}e_{i})=D{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j}).

Similarly, we have

\begin{matrix}e_{j}*\hat{D}(B)_{0}*e_{i}&=&e_{i}D(B)_{0}e_{j}\hfill&=&D(e_{j}B_{0}e_{i})\hfill\\
