M^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{s^{\prime}}}E^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{d^{\prime}}}N^{\prime}
is a triple of morphisms

(u,v,w)
in
{\cal C}
making the
following diagram commutative

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{s}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{d}}&N\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle u$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle w$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{s^{\prime}}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{d^{\prime}}}&N^{\prime}.\\
\end{matrix}

If
u
,
v
and
w
are isomorphisms,
(u,v,w)
is an
isomorphism of exact pairs
.
If there is such an isomorphism with
u=1_{M}
and
w=1_{N}
, the exact pairs
are called
equivalent
.

Once we have fixed a class of exact pairs
\cal E
closed under isomorphisms,
if
(s,d)\in\cal E
,
s
is called an
inflation
and
d
is called a

deflation
.

Lemma 10.7
.

Assume that
({\cal A},I)
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra. Then,
whenever

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is an exact sequence in
A/I\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, we have that
M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (f^{0},0)\ }}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (g^{0},0)\ }}N
is an exact pair in
({\cal A},I)\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Proof.

Write
f=(f^{0},0)
and
g=(g^{0},0)
.
We only prove that
f={\rm Ker}\,g
(the equality
g={\rm Cok}\,f

is proved dually). So suppose that
Z\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{t}}E

satisfies
gt=0
in
({\cal A},I)\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
Then,
g^{0}t^{0}=0
and
g^{0}t^{1}(v)=0
, for all
v\in V
. Then, there
exist a unique
h^{0}\in{\rm Hom}_{R}(Z,M)
with
f^{0}h^{0}=t^{0}
and, for each
v\in V
, there exists a unique
h^{1}(v)\in{\rm Hom}_{k}(Z,M)
with

f^{0}h^{1}(v)=t^{1}(v)
. Since
f^{0}
is an injective morphism of
A
-modules,
we get that
h^{1}\in{\rm Hom}_{A\hbox{-}A}(V,{\rm Hom}_{k}(Z,M))
.
Moreover, for
a\in A
and
z\in Z
,

f^{0}(ah^{0}(z))=at^{0}(z)=t^{0}(az)+t^{1}(\delta(a))(z)=f^{0}\left[h^{0}(az)+h^{1}(\delta(a))(z)\right].

Using again that
f^{0}
is injective, we get that

h=(h^{0},h^{1})\in{\rm Hom}_{({\cal A},I)}(Z,M)
. Clearly,
h
is unique such that
fh=t
in
({\cal A},I)\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
∎

For the sake of completeness, we recall the following definition.

Definition 10.8
.

Assume that
{\cal C}
is an additive
k
-category and
{\cal E}
is a class of exact pairs in
{\cal C}

closed under isomorphisms.
Then, the class
{\cal E}
is called
an exact structure
on

{\cal C}
iff

(1)

The composition of deflations is a deflation.

(2)

For each morphism
f:Z^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
and each deflation

d:Y\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
there exists a morphism
f^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Y
and a
deflation
d^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z^{\prime}
such that
df^{\prime}=fd^{\prime}
.

(3)

Identities are deflations. If
gf
is a deflation, then
so is
g
.

(3)
op

Identities are inflations. If
gf
is an inflation, then
so is
f
.

An
exact category
is a category
{\cal C}
, as above, endowed with an exact structure
{\cal E}
.
A functor
F:{\cal C}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal C}^{\prime}
, where the categories
{\cal C}
and
{\cal C}^{\prime}
are endowed with exact structures
{\cal E}
and
{\cal E}^{\prime}
, respectively, is called
exact
if it maps
{\cal E}
into
{\cal E}^{\prime}
.

Definition 10.9
.

Assume that
\underline{\cal A}=({\cal A},I)
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra
with layer
(R,W)
. Consider the class

\underline{\cal E}=\underline{\cal E}({\underline{\cal A}})
of composable pairs of morphisms
M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}N
such that there is a commutative diagram in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\varphi^{0},0)}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

such that
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\varphi^{0}}}E^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\psi^{0}}}N^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is a split exact sequence in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

We will establish that
\underline{\cal E}
is an exact structure of

\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
,
for any Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}
. The following statement gives a simple description of the class
\underline{\cal E}
.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
\underline{\cal A}
be a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra. Then,
\underline{\cal E}
is a class of exact pairs in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
closed under isomorphisms. Moreover, if we consider morphisms
f:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E
and
g:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. Then:

1.

M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}N
is a conflation iff
gf=0
and

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is a split exact sequence in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

2.

g:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
is a deflation iff
g^{0}:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

3.

f:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E
is an inflation iff
f^{0}:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E
is a section in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Proof.

We will prove (1) and, at the same time, that

\underline{\cal E}
is a class of exact pairs in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. If
M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}N\in\underline{\cal E}
, there is a commutative diagram in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\varphi^{0},0)}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

such that
r,s,t
are isomorphisms and
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\varphi^{0}}}E^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\psi^{0}}}N^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is a split exact sequence in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
In particular, we have the commutative diagram in
R
-Mod:

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle t^{0}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s^{0}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r^{0}$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\varphi^{0}}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\psi^{0}}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

where
r^{0},s^{0},t^{0}
are isomorphisms, which implies that

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is a split exact sequence in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Conversely, if
M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}N
is a pair of composable morphisms in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
such that
gf=0
and

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

is a split exact sequence in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, then, by (
10.2
)(1), there is a commutative square in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r$}}$\hss}\\
E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\\
\end{matrix}

where
r,s
are isomorphisms. It follows that
\psi^{0}
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. Now, consider the kernel
\varphi^{0}:M^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E^{\prime}
of the morphism
\psi^{0}:E^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N^{\prime}
in
A/I\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. Since
gf=0
, from (
10.7
), we know that there is an induced morphism of
\underline{\cal A}
-modules
t:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes:

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\varphi^{0},0)}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

where the lower row is an exact pair in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. In particular, we have a commutative diagram

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle t^{0}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s^{0}$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r^{0}$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\varphi^{0}}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\psi^{0}}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

which implies that
t^{0}
is an isomorphism. Since
\underline{\cal A}
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra,
t
is an isomorphism too and

M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}N
is an exact pair of
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, which belongs to
\underline{\cal E}
.

(2): If
g:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
is a deflation, clearly, we get that
g^{0}
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. On the other hand, if we start from a morphism
g:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
such that
g^{0}:E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, since
\underline{\cal A}
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra, by (
10.2
)(1),
there is a commutative square in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r$}}$\hss}\\
E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\\
\end{matrix}

where
r,s
are isomorphisms. Then, as before, we consider the kernel
\varphi^{0}
of
\psi^{0}
in
A/I\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and construct a commutative square in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f}}&E&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g}}&N\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle t$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle s$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle r$}}$\hss}\\
M^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\varphi^{0},0)}}&E^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(\psi^{0},0)}}&N^{\prime},\end{matrix}

where
t
is an isomorphism, so
g
is a deflation.
The proof of (3) is dual to the proof of (2).
∎

Proposition 10.11
.

If
\underline{\cal A}
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra, then

\underline{\cal E}
is an exact structure on
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.

Proof.

From (
10.10
), items (1), (3) and (3)
op
of Definition (
10.6
) are clear. Let us prove the remaining one (2). Take a
morphism
f:Z^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
and any deflation
d:Y\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
.
Consider the morphism
(f,d):Z^{\prime}\bigoplus Y\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
in
({\cal A},I)\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
. Since
d
is a deflation,
d^{0}
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
and, therefore, so is
(f^{0},d^{0})
. Then,
(f,d):Z^{\prime}\bigoplus Y\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z
is a deflation and its kernel has the form

(d^{\prime},-f^{\prime})^{t}:Y^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Z^{\prime}\bigoplus Y
.
Therefore, we have
fd^{\prime}=df^{\prime}
and a pull-back diagram in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}

\begin{matrix}Y^{\prime}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(d^{\prime})^{0}}}&Z^{\prime}\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle(f^{\prime})^{0}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle f^{0}$}}$\hss}\\
Y&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{d^{0}}}&Z.\\
\end{matrix}

Since
d^{0}
is a retraction in
R\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
, the same is true for
(d^{\prime})^{0}
. Therefore,
d^{\prime}
is a deflation, proving our statement.
∎

The preceding result applies to the module category of the Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra

\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)=({\cal A}(\Delta),I)
constructed before, see (
4.19
).
The following theorem combines the equivalence of (
6.3
)
with the one constructed in (
8.3
). It generalizes the result given in
[
9
]
for the case of quasi-hereditary algebras. It relies on the explicit description of the equivalence of categories
{\cal F}(\Delta)\simeq H^{0}({\rm tw}(A))
mentioned in (
6.3
). The detailed proof of this result is beyond the scope of this paper.

Theorem 10.12
.

Consider the equivalence functors
K:{\cal F}(\Delta)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}H^{0}({\rm tw}(A))
of (
6.3
),
E:H^{0}({\rm tw}(A))\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}H^{-1}({\rm tw}(B))
, of (
5.8
),
and the equivalence
M:H^{-1}({\rm tw}(B))\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
constructed in (
8.3
).
Then the composition

G:=MEK:{\cal F}(\Delta)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

is an equivalence mapping short exact sequences to conflations and such that
G(\Delta_{i})\cong S_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
, where
S_{i}=(ke_{i},\rho_{S_{i}})
and
\rho_{S_{i}}=0
, with the notation of (
8.2
).

11
The right algebra of
({\cal A},I)

In this section we recall some results by W.L. Burt and M.C. Butler on bocses and reformulate them in our context for their application in the next section.

Remark 11.1
.

In this section we assume that
\underline{\cal A}=({\cal A},I)
is a
special triangular interlaced weak ditalgebra
, in the following sense. The weak ditalgebra

{\cal A}=(T,\delta)
admits a triangular layer
(S,W_{0}\oplus W_{1})
, where
S
is a finite product of fields. We write
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, where
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit of
S
as a sum of primitive orthogonal central idempotents. We adopt the notation of (
9.1
)–(
9.4
) with
R=S
. Thus, we have
T=T_{S}(W_{0}\oplus W_{1})
,
A=T_{S}(W_{0})
,
V=A\otimes_{S}W_{1}\otimes_{S}A
,
and
I
is an ideal of
A
. We will assume that
W_{0}
and
W_{1}
are finite dimensional
S
-
S
-bimodules and we will furthermore assume that the quotient algebra
\underline{A}:=A/I
is finite-dimensional.

Recall, from
[
1
]
(4.3), that

\underline{V}:=V/(IV+VI)=\frac{A\otimes_{S}W_{1}\otimes_{S}A}{I\otimes W_{1}\otimes A+A\otimes W_{1}\otimes I}\cong\underline{A}\otimes_{S}W_{1}\otimes_{S}\underline{A}.

We denote by
\pi=p\otimes id_{W_{1}}\otimes p:A\otimes W_{1}\otimes A\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{A}\otimes W_{1}\otimes\underline{A}
, where
p:A\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{A}
is the canonical projection.

The pair
\underline{\cal A}
is a Roiter interlaced weak ditalgebra, as in (
10.1
), and §
10
applies to its module category
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm Mod}
.
In particular, if we make
\underline{A}=A/I
, the embedding functor

L=L_{\underline{\cal A}}:\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod},

which maps each
\underline{A}
-module
M
onto itself and any morphism

f^{0}:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
onto
(f^{0},0):M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, is an exact functor, when we consider the exact structure
\underline{\cal E}
on
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
introduced in (
10.9
), see (
10.10
).

Definition 11.2
.

Assume that
\underline{\cal A}
is a special interlaced weak ditalgebra as above. Then, the
right algebra of
\underline{\cal A}
is the finite-dimensionl
k
-algebra

\Gamma:={\rm End}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A})^{op}.

There is an embedding of
\underline{A}
in
\Gamma
given by the composition

\begin{matrix}\underline{A}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\cong}}&{\rm End}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A})^{op}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{L_{\underline{\cal A}}}}&{\rm End}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A})^{op}&=&\Gamma\end{matrix},

so
\Gamma
becomes naturally an
\underline{A}
-algebra.

The statements in (
11.13
) outline important relations between the algebras
\underline{A}
and
\Gamma
, they translate results of W.L. Burt and M.C.R. Butler to the terminology we use here, see
[
5
]
and
[
6
]
. For the sake of completeness, we give a sketch-review of their proofs.
In the rest of this section,
\underline{\cal A}
is a special interlaced weak ditalgebra,
\Gamma
is its right algebra, and we adopt the preceding notation.

Lemma 11.3
.

For any
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, there is an exact sequence of left
\underline{A}
-modules

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A},M)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{L}}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\xi}}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

where the space
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)
is a left
\underline{A}
-module through
\underline{A}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm End}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A})^{op}
.

Proof.

Since
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, any
f^{1}\in{\rm Hom}_{A\hbox{-}A}(V,{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M))
determines an
\underline{f}^{1}\in{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}\hbox{-}\underline{A}}(\underline{V},{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M))
such that
\underline{f}^{1}\pi=f^{1}
. Then, we can define

\begin{matrix}\xi&:&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)\\
&&\hbox{ \ \ }f=(f^{0},f^{1})&\longmapsto&\xi(f):\underline{V}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\\
&&&&\hbox{\hskip 42.67912pt}v\mapsto\underline{f}^{1}(v)[1].\\
\end{matrix}

Thus,
\xi
is a morphism of left
\underline{A}
-modules. Suppose that

f\in{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)
satisfies that
\xi(f)=0
. Thus,

\underline{f}^{1}(v)[1]=0
, for all
v\in\underline{V}
. Hence,
\underline{f}^{1}(v)[b]=\underline{f}^{1}(vb)[1]=0
, for all
b\in\underline{A}
. Thus,
\underline{f}^{1}(v)=0
, for all
v\in\underline{V}
, and
\underline{f}^{1}=0
, thus
f^{1}=0
. This means that
f\in{\rm Im}\,L
. Therefore,

{\rm Im}\,L={\rm Ker}\,\xi
.

Take
g\in{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)
and consider its image
\underline{g}^{1}
under the canonical isomorphism

{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)\cong{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V}\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A},M)\cong{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}\hbox{-}\underline{A}}(\underline{V},{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M)).

Thus,
\underline{g}^{1}:\underline{V}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M)
is a morphism of
\underline{A}
-bimodules satisfying
\underline{g}^{1}(v)[b]=g(vb)
, for
v\in\underline{V}
and
b\in\underline{A}
. Consider the composition morphism
g^{1}:=\underline{g}^{1}\pi\in{\rm Hom}_{A\hbox{-}A}(V,{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M))
.
Then, fix any
m_{0}\in M
and define, for
a\in\underline{A}
,

g^{0}(a):=am_{0}-g^{1}(\delta(a))[1].

Thus,
g^{0}\in{\rm Hom}_{k}(\underline{A},M)
. Moreover,
(g^{0},g^{1})\in{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)
. Indeed, given
a\in\underline{A}
and
b\in A
, we have

\begin{matrix}bg^{0}(a)&=&bam_{0}-bg^{1}(\delta(a))[1]\hfill\\
&=&bam_{0}-g^{1}(b\delta(a))[1]\hfill\\
&=&bam_{0}+g^{1}(\delta(b)a-\delta(ba))[1]\hfill\\
&=&bam_{0}-g^{1}(\delta(ba))[1]+g^{1}(\delta(b))[a]\hfill\\
&=&g^{0}(ba)+g^{1}(\delta(b))[a].\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Since
\xi(g^{0},g^{1})(v)=\underline{g}^{1}(v)[1]=g(v)
, for all
v\in\underline{V}
,
\xi
is surjective and we are done.
∎

Remark 11.4
.

Using the same argument in the proof of
[
3
]
(6.20), we obtain that any projective
\underline{A}
-module is an
\underline{\cal E}
-projective
\underline{\cal A}
-module. Moreover, every

\underline{\cal E}
-projective
\underline{\cal A}
-module is isomorphic to
a direct summand in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
of some projective
\underline{A}
-module.

Then, the functor
F={\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},-):\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
is exact.
The following lemma implies that the right
\underline{A}
-module
\Gamma
is flat. Indeed, the functor
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
is exact because

\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-\cong FL
and we know that
L
and
F
are exact functors, see (
10.7
).

It is not hard to see that the functor
F
is in fact full and faithful. The proof of
[
3
]
(7.12) can be adapted to this situation.

Lemma 11.5
.

For
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, the map
\sigma_{M}:\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)
given by
\sigma(f\otimes m)=f_{m}=(f^{0}_{m},f^{1}_{m})
, where

f^{0}_{m}(a)=f^{0}(a)m
and
f^{1}_{m}(v)[a]=f^{1}(v)[a]m
defines a natural isomorphism

\sigma_{M}:\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},L(M)).

Proof.

Notice that

\sigma_{\underline{A}}:\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}\rightarrow\Gamma
is just the isomorphism given
by multiplication. From the additivity of the
functors
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
and

{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},L(-))
, we obtain that

\sigma_{\underline{A}^{n}}
is an isomorphism too. Finally, if we consider a
free resolution
\underline{A}^{m}\rightarrow\underline{A}^{n}\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0
of

M
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, we can apply the exact functors
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
and

{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},L(-))
to it, and then
\sigma_{M}
is an isomorphism because
the following diagram commutes

\begin{matrix}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}^{m}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}^{n}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0\hfill\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma_{\underline{A}^{m}}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma_{\underline{A}^{n}}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma_{M}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\hfill\\
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{m})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},\underline{A}^{n})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0.\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

∎

Lemma 11.6
.

The functor
T:=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-:\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
admits as a right adjoint the restriction functor
S:\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Consider the adjunction isomorphism

\zeta:{\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(TM,N)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(M,SN),

and its unit
\alpha
, that is the natural transformation

\alpha:1_{\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}ST
defined, for
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, by
\alpha_{M}:=\zeta(1_{TM}):M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}STM
. Then, for each
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, we have the commutative square

\begin{matrix}M&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\alpha_{M}}}&\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\psi_{M}$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sigma_{M}$}}$\hss}\\
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A},M)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{L}}&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},M),\\
\end{matrix}\\

where
\psi_{M}
is the canonical isomorphism and
\sigma_{M}
is the isomorphism introduced in (
11.5
). In particular,
\alpha_{M}
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

For
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and
N\in\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, we have the adjunction isomorphism

\begin{matrix}\zeta&:&{\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(TM,N)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(M,SN)\\
&&h&\longmapsto&\zeta(h):M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N\\
&&&&\hbox{\hskip 51.21504pt}m\mapsto h(1\otimes m),\\
\end{matrix}\\

with inverse

\begin{matrix}\zeta^{\prime}&:&{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(M,SN)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&{\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(TM,N)\\
&&g&\longmapsto&p(1_{\Gamma}\otimes g),\\
\end{matrix}\\

where
p:\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N
is the product morphism.
The verification of the naturality of
\zeta
is straightforward.

Notice that
\alpha_{M}(m)=1\otimes m
, for
m\in M
. Make
\theta:=\sigma_{M}\alpha_{M}
. Then, we have

\theta(m)=\sigma_{M}\alpha_{M}(m)=\sigma_{M}(id_{\underline{A}}\otimes m)=(\theta(m)^{0},\theta(m)^{1})
, where
\theta(m)^{0}[a]=am
and

\theta(m)^{1}(v)[a]=id_{\underline{A}}^{1}(v)[a]m=0
. Then,
\sigma_{M}\alpha_{M}(m)=\theta(m)=(\psi_{M}(m),0)=L\psi_{M}(m)
, and we are done.
∎

Corollary 11.7
.

For each
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, there is the following exact sequence in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ \alpha_{M}\ \ }}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ \ }}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0.

Proof.

It follows from (
11.3
) and (
11.6
).
∎

Definition 11.8
.

An
\underline{A}
-bimodule
\underline{V}
is called
projectivizing
iff
\underline{V}\otimes_{\underline{A}}X
is projective for all
X\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and
Y\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{V}
is projective for all
Y\in{\rm mod}\hbox{-}\underline{A}
.

Lemma 11.9
.

With our fixed notation, the
S
-
S
-bimodule
W_{1}
is freely generated by a set
\mathbb{B}_{1}
. Hence, the
\underline{A}
-bimodule
\underline{V}
is projectivizing.

Proof.

Since
S
is a finite product of fields and
W_{1}
is finite-dimensional, there is a directed
k
-basis
\mathbb{B}_{1}
of
W_{1}
and an isomorphism

W_{1}\cong\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{B}_{1}}Se_{t(w)}\otimes_{k}e_{s(w)}S.

Then, we have

\begin{matrix}\underline{V}&\cong&\underline{A}\otimes_{S}W_{1}\otimes_{S}\underline{A}\hfill\\
&\cong&\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{B}_{1}}\underline{A}\otimes_{S}Se_{t(w)}\otimes_{k}e_{s(w)}S\otimes_{S}\underline{A}\hfill\\
&\cong&\bigoplus_{w\in\mathbb{B}_{1}}\underline{A}e_{t(w)}\otimes_{k}e_{s(w)}\underline{A}\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Now notice that, given a family
\{Q_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
of projective left
\underline{A}
-modules and a family
\{P_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
of projective right
\underline{A}
-modules, then the
\underline{A}
-bimodule

\underline{V}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}{P_{i}}\otimes_{k}Q_{i}
is projectivizing.
Thus, the
\underline{A}
-bimodule
\underline{V}
is projectivizing.
∎

Corollary 11.10
.

The right
\underline{A}
-module
\Gamma
is projective.

Proof.

Taking
M=\underline{A}
, from (
11.3
), we have the exact sequence of vector spaces

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A},\underline{A})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{L}}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},\underline{A})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\xi}}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},\underline{A})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0.

Thus, we have the exact sequence of vector spaces

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{A}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{s}}\Gamma\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\xi}}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},\underline{A})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

where
s
is the canonical embedding. This is, in fact, an exact sequence of right
\underline{A}
-modules. Since the left
\underline{A}
-module
\underline{V}
is finitely generated projective, its dual right
\underline{A}
-module

{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},\underline{A})
is finitely generated projective, thus the last exact sequence splits, and
\Gamma_{\underline{A}}
is projective.
∎

From
[
5
]
(3.6) or
[
6
]
(11.8), we have the following.

Lemma 11.11
.

If
\underline{V}
is a projectivizing
\underline{A}
-bimodule, then

{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{V},M)
is an injective
\underline{A}
-module, for all
M\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
.

Proposition 11.12
.

Consider the tensor functor
T:=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-:\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Then, every extension
e:0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}TM\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}TN\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0
in
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
is equivalent to an extension
Te^{\prime}
obtained by applying
T
to an extension

e^{\prime\prime}:0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E^{\prime\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
.

Proof.

We have the adjoint pair of exact functors

\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\ \smash{\mathop{\raise 3.0pt\hbox to40.0pt{\leftarrowfill}\hskip-40.0pt\lower 3.0pt\hbox to40.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{S}_{T}}\ \Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod},

where
S
is the restriction functor. Moreover, the morphism
\alpha_{M}:M\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}STM
is a monomorphism with injective cokernel. Then, we can follow the argument of
[
6
]
(10.3) to obtain the wanted extension equivalent to
e
.
∎

Proposition 11.13
.

Given a special interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}
, we have:

1.

The functor

F={\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},-):\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

is a full and faithful exact functor.

2.

The
\underline{A}
-algebra
\Gamma
is a right
\underline{A}
-module by restriction, it determines an exact functor

\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-:\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}.

and, we have
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-\cong{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(\underline{A},L(-))
.

3.

The functor
F
restricts to an equivalence of categories
F:\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal I}
, where
{\cal I}
is the full subcategory of
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
of modules induced from
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, that is by the class of
\Gamma
-modules isomorphic to some

\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N
, for some
N\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
.

Moreover, the subcategory
{\cal I}
of
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
is closed under extensions.

4.

The functor
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-:\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
induces epimorphisms

{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,N)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M,\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N),

for all
M,N\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and
n\geq 1
. They are isomorphisms for
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

(1) and (2) were remarked in (
11.4
), and (3) is (
11.12
).

(4): Recall that, we have an exact sequence
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\alpha_{N}}}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}I\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, with
I
injective, for any
N\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. The corresponding long exact sequence gives epimorphisms

{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,\alpha_{N}):{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,N)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N),

for
n\geq 1
and isomorphisms for
n\geq 2
. We have a commutative diagram

\begin{matrix}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,N)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\gamma^{n}}}&{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M,\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N)\hfill\\
\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(M,\alpha_{N})$}}$}\Bigg\downarrow&&\Bigg\uparrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M,\beta_{TN})$}}$\hss}\\
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(_{\underline{A}}M,_{\underline{A}}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N)&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\gamma^{n}}}&{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M,\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N),\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

where
\gamma^{n}
denotes the morphisms induced by the application of the exact functor
T=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
, and

\beta_{TN}:\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}N
is the product map. Since the composition
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M,\beta_{TN})\gamma^{n}
is
an isomorphism, see
[
6
]
(20.9), we are done.
∎

12
Strict interlaced weak ditalgebras

Definition 12.1
.

Let
{\cal P}=({\cal P},\leq)
be a finite preordered set. Let
\underline{\cal A}=({\cal A},I)
be a triangular interlaced weak ditalgebra, thus
{\cal A}=(T_{S}(W_{0}\oplus W_{1}),\delta)
is a weak ditalgebra with triangular layer
(S,W_{0}\oplus W_{1})
and
I
is an
{\cal A}
-triangular ideal of
A:=T_{S}(W_{0})
. We will say that
\underline{\cal A}
is
{\cal P}
-
strict interlaced weak ditalgebra
iff the following holds:

1.

The algebra
S
is a finite product of
|{\cal P}|
copies of the field
k
, thus
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, where
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit of
S
as a sum of primitive orthogonal central idempotents.

2.

We have that
e_{j}W_{1}e_{i}\not=0
implies
i\leq j
,

3.

We have that
e_{j}W_{0}e_{i}\not=0
implies
\overline{i}<\overline{j}
.

4.

I\subseteq{\rm rad}(A)^{2}
.

We will denote by
\underline{A}
the quotient
k
-algebra
A/I
.

Remark 12.2
.

If
\underline{\cal A}
be a
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced weak ditalgebra as above,
we will identify the graded tensor algebra
A=T_{S}(W_{0})
with the path algebra
k({\cal B}_{0})
of the quiver
{\cal B}_{0}
with set of points
{\cal P}
; such that for
i,j\in{\cal P}
, the set of solid arrows from
i
to
j
is a fixed basis
\mathbb{B}_{0}(i,j)
of the vector space
e_{j}W_{0}e_{i}
.

Notice that the quiver
{\cal B}_{0}
coincides with the Gabiel quiver of
\underline{A}
and has not oriented cycles. Then, we have the partial order of precedence
\preceq
in
{\cal P}
, as defined in (
1.10
).
So, for
i,j\in{\cal P}
, we have
i\prec j
iff there is a non-trivial path from
i
to
j
in the quiver
{\cal B}_{0}
. In fact, we always have that
i\prec j
implies
\overline{i}<\overline{j}
.

Since the quiver
{\cal B}_{0}
has no oriented cycle, the algebra
\underline{A}
is finite-dimensional. Let us write
\underline{P}_{i}:=\underline{A}e_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. Then, the family
\{\underline{P}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a complete family of representatives of the indecomposable projective
\underline{A}
-modules (we denote by
\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
the corresponding family of simple
\underline{A}
-modules
S_{i}=\underline{P}_{i}/{\rm rad}(\underline{P}_{i})
, which is a complete family of representatives of the isoclasses of the simple
\underline{A}
-modules).

Lemma 12.3
.

Assume that
\underline{\cal A}
is a
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced weak ditalgebra, then the algebra
\underline{A}
is directed.
Moreover, the
triple
\underline{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is a strict homological system for the algebra
\underline{A}
.

Proof.

From (
1.13
), we already know that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\preceq,\{S_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is a strict homological system for
\underline{A}
. Since
i\prec j
implies
\overline{i}<\overline{j}
, we immediately obtain that
\underline{\cal H}
is a strict homological system for
\underline{A}
.
∎

Remark 12.4
.

Let
\underline{\cal A}
be a
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced weak ditalgebra and adopt the notation of (
12.1
). Then, we have a special interlaced weak ditalgebra and the results of sections §
10
and §
11
hold.

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
\underline{\cal A}
be a
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced ditalgebra. Then, there is a family
\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
of non-isomorphic indecomposable
{\underline{\cal E}}
-projective
\underline{\cal A}
-modules, which is a complete family of representatives of the indecomposable
\underline{\cal E}
-projective
\underline{\cal A}
-modules and, moreover, for each
i\in{\cal P}
, we have
an isomorphism in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

\underline{P}_{i}\cong Q_{i}\oplus(\bigoplus_{i\prec j}m_{i,j}Q_{j}),\hbox{ for some }m_{i,j}\geq 0.

Proof.

Given
i\in{\cal P}
, as we remarked in (
11.4
), the
\underline{\cal A}
-module
\underline{P}_{i}
is
{\underline{\cal E}}
-projective. Consider its decomposition as a direct sum of indecomposables. So we have an isomorphism in

\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
:

g=(g^{0},g^{1}):\underline{P}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n_{i}}Q_{i,j},

a direct sum of indecomposable
\underline{\cal E}
-projectives.
Then, we have the linear isomorphism
g_{i}^{0}:e_{i}\underline{A}e_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n_{i}}e_{i}Q_{i,j}
, where the domain is one dimensional. Then, there is a unique
Q_{i}:=Q_{i,j}
with
\dim e_{i}Q_{i,j}=1
. We shall prove our lemma by induction on the partial order
\prec
on
{\cal P}
.

If
i
is maximal (that is if
i
is a sink in
{\cal B}_{0}
), then
\underline{P}_{i}\cong Q_{i}
is indecomposable in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and we are done. Assume we have
\underline{P}_{s}\cong Q_{s}\oplus(\bigoplus_{s\prec t}m_{s,t}Q_{t})
, for all
s\in{\cal P}
with
i\prec s
, for a fixed
i\in{\cal P}
. Consider the indecomposable decomposition of
\underline{P}_{i}\cong Q_{i}\oplus(\oplus_{j=1}^{u_{i}}Q_{i,j})
of
\underline{P}_{i}
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Fix any direct summand
Q:=Q_{i,j}
, not isomorphic to
Q_{i}
, then
Q
is an indecomposable

\underline{\cal E}
-projective
\underline{\cal A}
-module such that
e_{i}Q=0
. Moreover, if
e_{j}Q\not=0
, we have
e_{j}\underline{A}e_{i}\not=0
, so
i\prec j
. Now, consider the projective cover
q:\underline{P}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Q
of
Q
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, then we have the commutative diagram

\begin{matrix}\underline{P}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{q}}&Q\\
&\hskip-5.0pt\mathop{\searrow\hskip-6.0pt\raise 5.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\overline{q}$}}&\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}$\hss}\\
&&Q/{\rm rad}(Q).\\
\end{matrix}

There, the module
\underline{P}_{j}
is a direct summand of
\underline{P}
iff
S_{j}
is a direct summand of
Q/{\rm rad}(Q)
, which is only possible if
e_{j}Q\not=0
, that is if
i\prec j
. This means that

\underline{P}\cong\oplus_{i\prec j}s_{j}\underline{P}_{j}
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, for some
s_{j}\geq 0
. Then, if
\sigma:K\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{P}
is the kernel of
q
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, we have the conflation

K\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (\sigma,0)\ }}\underline{P}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (q,0)\ }}Q

in the exact category
(\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod},{\underline{\cal E}})
, where
Q
is an
{\underline{\cal E}}
-projective module. Hence,
Q
is a direct summand of
\underline{P}\cong\oplus_{i\prec j}s_{j}\underline{P}_{j}
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Then, by induction hypothesis,
Q\cong Q_{t}
, for some
i\prec j\prec t
, and this finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 12.6
.

Let
\underline{\cal A}
be a
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced weak ditalgebra and consider its right algebra
\Gamma
. For
i\in{\cal P}
, make
P^{\prime}_{i}:=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q_{i}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}:=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i}
. Then,
\{P^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a complete family of representatives of the indecomposable projective
\Gamma
-modules and

{\cal H}^{\prime}:=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in P})
is a strict homological system for
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Since
F
is full and faithful, each
P^{\prime}_{i}=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q_{i}\cong F(L(Q_{i}))=F(Q_{i})
and
each
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i}\cong F(L(S_{i}))=F(S_{i})
are indecomposable
\Gamma
-modules.
Moreover, using again that
F
is full and faithful, and (
12.5
), we know that
\{P^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
and
\{\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
are families of non-isomorphic
\Gamma
-modules.

From (
12.5
), we know that
\Gamma\cong\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{A}\cong\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}(\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\underline{A}e_{i})\cong F(\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}\underline{P}_{i})\cong\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}F(Q_{i})\oplus(\bigoplus_{i\prec j}m_{i,j}F(Q_{j}))
, so every
P^{\prime}_{i}
is a projective indecomposable
\Gamma
-module, and
\{P^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a complete family of representatives of the indecomposable projective
\Gamma
-modules.

Assume that, for
i,j\in{\cal P}
, we have
{\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i},\Delta^{\prime}_{j})\not=0
. Then, we have that
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
. If we assume that
i\not=j
, and take
0\not=f=(f^{0},f^{1})\in{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\cal A}}(S_{i},S_{j})
, we have that
f^{0}=0
, but
0\not=f^{1}\in{\rm Hom}_{S\hbox{-}S}(W_{1},{\rm Hom}_{k}(S_{i},S_{j}))\cong e_{j}W_{1}e_{i}
, therefore, since
\underline{\cal A}
is strict, we obtain that
i\leq j
.

Now, suppose that, for
i,j\in{\cal P}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Gamma}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i},\Delta^{\prime}_{j})\not=0
. From (
11.13
), we obtain
{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\underline{A}}(S_{i},S_{j})\not=0
and, from (
12.3
), we get
\overline{i}<\overline{j}
.

So, once we show that
{\cal H}^{\prime}
is an admissible homological system for the algebra
\Gamma
, we get that it is strict.

Fix
i\in{\cal P}
and consider the exact sequence
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm rad}(\underline{P}_{i})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{P}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{p^{0}}}S_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0
in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Applying
L_{\underline{\cal A}}
, we have a conflation

{\rm rad}(\underline{P}_{i})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{P}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}S_{i}
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Consider the following composition of morphisms in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

\begin{matrix}g_{i}=(g_{i}^{0},g_{i}^{1})&=&(Q_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (\sigma_{i},0)\ }}&Q_{i}\oplus(\bigoplus_{i\prec j}m_{i,j}Q_{j})&\cong&\underline{P}_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (p^{0},0)\ }}&S_{i}).\end{matrix}

Since
g_{i}^{0}\not=0
, we get that
g_{i}^{0}
is a retraction, so
g_{i}
is a deflation and we have a conflation

K_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{i}}}Q_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g_{i}}}S_{i}
in
\underline{\cal E}
. Here, we know that
e_{j}K_{i}\not=0
implies
e_{j}Q_{i}\not=0
, hence that
i\prec j
.
By the definition of
\underline{\cal E}
in (
10.9
), we know there is a commutative diagram

\begin{matrix}K_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f_{i}}}&Q_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g_{i}}}&S_{i}\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}\\
K^{\prime}_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (u_{i}^{0},0)\ }}&E^{\prime}_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ (v_{i}^{0},0)\ }}&S^{\prime}_{i},\\
\end{matrix}

where
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}K^{\prime}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{u_{i}^{0}}}E^{\prime}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{v_{i}^{0}}}S^{\prime}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0
is exact in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
.
Applying
F
to the diagram, from (
11.13
)(2), we get the commutative diagram in
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
with exact lower row

\begin{matrix}F(K_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{F(f_{i})}}&F(Q_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{F(g_{i})}}&F(S_{i})\\
\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}&&\Bigg\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cong$}}$\hss}\\
F(K^{\prime}_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ F(u_{i}^{0},0)\ }}&F(E^{\prime}_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\ F(v_{i}^{0},0)\ }}&F(S^{\prime}_{i}).\\
\end{matrix}

Hence, we obtain an exact sequence of
\Gamma
-modules

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}K_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

where
P^{\prime}_{i}=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q_{i}
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i}
.

Now, we show that
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}K_{i}
is filtered by
\Gamma
-modules
\Delta^{\prime}_{j}
with
\overline{i}<\overline{j}
.
Recall that the
\underline{A}
-module
K_{i}
admits a simple
\underline{A}
-module
S_{j}
as a composition factor iff
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{A}}(\underline{A}e_{j},K_{i})\not=0
, that is iff
e_{j}K_{i}\not=0
, thus
i\prec j
. Consider a composition series
0\subseteq E_{\ell}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq E_{1}\subseteq E_{0}=K_{i}
of the
\underline{A}
-module
K_{i}
, then we have exact sequences

\begin{matrix}0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{\ell}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{\ell-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}S_{j_{2}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0\\
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{\ell-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{\ell-2}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}S_{j_{3}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0\\
\cdots\\
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}E_{0}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}S_{j_{\ell}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0\\
\end{matrix}

with
i\prec j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}
and
E_{\ell}\cong S_{j_{1}}
. Applying the exact functor
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
we obtain exact sequences

\begin{matrix}0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{j_{1}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}E_{\ell-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{j_{2}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0\\
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}E_{\ell-1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}E_{\ell-2}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{j_{3}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0\\
\cdots\\
0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}E_{1}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}E_{0}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{j_{\ell}}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0.\\
\end{matrix}

Then, the module
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}K_{i}
is filtered by
\Gamma
-modules
\Delta^{\prime}_{j}
with
i\prec j_{1},\ldots,j_{\ell}
, thus with
\overline{i}<\overline{j_{1}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\ell}}
. Thus,
P^{\prime}_{i}\in{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
, and
{\cal H}^{\prime}
is a strict homological system.
∎

Lemma 12.7
.

Adopt the notation of (
11.13
). Then, we have
{\cal I}={\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
. Moreover, the family
\{P^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a complete family of representatives of the indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
-projective objects.

Proof.

Notice first that since
{\cal I}
is closed under extensions and
\Delta^{\prime}_{i}=\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i}\in{\cal I}
, an easy induction shows that
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})\subseteq{\cal I}
. Conversely, given
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M\in{\cal I}
, a composition series for the
\underline{A}
-module
M
is transformed by the application of
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}-
into a
\Delta^{\prime}
-filtration of
\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}M
, so it belongs to
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
.
So, indeed we have
{\cal I}={\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
.

From (
12.6
), we know that
each
P^{\prime}_{i}\in{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
, and it is an indecomposable projective
\Gamma
-module, so, it is an indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
-projective. Assume that
Q^{\prime}\in{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
is an indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
-projective. Since
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
consists of induced
\Gamma
-modules, we know that
Q^{\prime}\cong\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q
, for some
Q\in\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Consider any exact sequence in
\underline{A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}K\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{f^{0}}}\underline{P}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{g^{0}}}Q\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0

with
\underline{P}
a projective
\underline{A}
-module.
Then, we have the conflation

K\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(f^{0},0)}}\underline{P}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{(g^{0},0)}}Q
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
and an exact sequence

0\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}K\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{P}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}Q^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}0,

in
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, which lies in
{\cal I}={\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
. Moreover, we know that
the projective
\underline{A}
-module
\underline{P}
is
\underline{\cal E}
-projective and, so, it has the form
\underline{P}\cong\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}m_{i}Q_{i}
in
\underline{\cal A}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
. Then,

\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}\underline{P}\cong\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}m_{i}\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}Q_{i}=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}m_{i}P^{\prime}_{i}
. Since
Q^{\prime}
is an indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
-projective, the preceding exact sequence splits and we obtain that
Q^{\prime}\cong P^{\prime}_{i}
, for some
i\in{\cal P}

∎

Theorem 12.8
.

Assume that
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
is an admissible homological system for
\Lambda
. Then, the following are equivalent.

1.

The Yoneda algebra
A(\Delta)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}{\rm Ext}^{n}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta)
admits a strict structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra over
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
.

2.

The homological system
{\cal H}
is strict.

Moreover, in this case the algebra
\Lambda
and the right algebra
\Gamma
of the interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
of
\Delta

are Morita equivalent.

Proof.

(2)
implies
(1)
: If
{\cal H}
is a strict homological system, from (
3.7
), we know that the Yoneda algebra of
\Delta
admits a strict structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra over
S
.

(1)
implies
(2)
: The construction of the triangular interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)=({\cal A}(\Delta),I)
accomplished in (
4.19
), only required an admissible homological system
{\cal H}
for
\Lambda
and a strict structure of
A_{\infty}
-algebra on the Yoneda algebra of
\Delta
, as we assumed at the begining of §4. It is clear that the resulting interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
is
{\cal P}
-strict. Then, we have the strict homological system
{\cal H}^{\prime}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
for its right algebra
\Gamma
described in (
12.6
).

We have the exact functor
F:\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
such that
F(S_{i})=\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. From (
11.13
) and (
12.7
), we know that this functor restricts to an equivalence of categories
F^{\prime}:\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
, which maps conflations onto short exact sequences.

From (
10.12
), we know that there is an equivalence of categories

G:{\cal F}(\Delta)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)\hbox{-}{\rm mod},\hbox{ with }G(\Delta_{i})\cong S_{i},\hbox{ for }i\in{\cal P},

and such that it maps short exact sequences onto conflations.
The equivalence
\Theta=F^{\prime}G:{\cal F}(\Delta)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
, satisfies
\Theta(\Delta_{i})\cong\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
, and it maps short exact sequences with terms in
{\cal F}(\Delta)
onto short exact sequences with terms in
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
. Its quasi-inverse
\Theta^{\prime}:{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal F}(\Delta)
satisfies
\Theta^{\prime}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\cong\Delta_{i}
, for
i\in{\cal P}
.

We clearly have that
\{P_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a family of non-isomorphic indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta)
-projectives. Since
\Theta^{\prime}
is an equivalence, from (
12.7
), we know that
\{\Theta^{\prime}(P^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i\in{\cal P}}
is a complete family of non-isomorphic indecomposable
{\cal F}(\Delta)
-projectives. Suppose that
P_{i}\cong\Theta^{\prime}(P^{\prime}_{j})
, thus
\Theta(P_{i})\cong\Theta\Theta^{\prime}(P^{\prime}_{j})\cong P^{\prime}_{j}
. From (
1.4
), we have the exact sequence in
{\cal F}(\Delta)

\begin{matrix}0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&Q_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&P_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\nu_{i}}}&\Delta_{i}&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0.\end{matrix}

Thus, we have an exact sequence in
{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})

\begin{matrix}0&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Theta(Q_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Theta(P_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&\Theta(\Delta_{i})&\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}&0.\end{matrix}

This gives a surjective morphism
P^{\prime}_{j}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
in
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
, which has to factor through the projective cover
\nu_{i}^{\prime}:P^{\prime}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
, see again (
1.4
). Then, there is a surjective morphism
P^{\prime}_{i}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}P^{\prime}_{j}
and, so, we get
P^{\prime}_{i}\cong P^{\prime}_{j}
, and
i=j
. So we have that
\Theta^{\prime}(P^{\prime}_{i})\cong P_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

Thus the equivalence
\Theta:{\cal F}(\Delta)\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})
is such that
\Theta(\Delta_{i})\cong\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
and
\Theta(P_{i})\cong P^{\prime}_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. After adjusting
\Theta
, if necessary, we can assume that
\Theta(\Delta_{i})=\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
and
\Theta(P_{i})=P^{\prime}_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. Make
P:=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P_{i}
and
P^{\prime}:=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P^{\prime}_{i}
, then
\Theta
determines an isomorphism of
k
-algebras

\Lambda^{\prime}:={\rm End}_{\Lambda}(P)^{op}={\rm End}_{\Lambda}(\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P_{i})^{op}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\theta}}{\rm End}_{\Gamma}(\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}P^{\prime}_{i})^{op}={\rm End}_{\Gamma}(P^{\prime})^{op}=:\Gamma^{\prime}.

We know that
\Gamma
is Morita equivalent to
\Gamma^{\prime}
and that
\Lambda
is Morita quivalent to
\Lambda^{\prime}
.
Consider the following series of equivalences

\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\Phi}}\Gamma^{\prime}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{F_{\theta}}}\Lambda^{\prime}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\leftarrowfill}}\limits^{\Psi}}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}

where
\Phi={\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(P^{\prime},-)
,
\Psi={\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P,-)
, and
F_{\theta}
is the restriction functor determined by the isomorphism
\theta
. Once the equivalence
\Theta
has been fixed as above, we can choose wisely a quasi-inverse
\Theta^{\prime}:{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\cal F}(\Delta)
of
\Theta
such that
\Theta^{\prime}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})=\Delta_{i}
,
\Theta^{\prime}(P^{\prime}_{i})=P_{i}
, for
i\in{\cal P}
and, furthermore, such that
\Theta^{\prime}\Theta(\lambda^{\prime})=\lambda^{\prime}
, for any
\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
.

We claim that
F_{\theta}\Phi(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\cong\Psi(\Delta_{i})
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
. Indeed, we have

\Phi(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})={\rm Hom}_{\Gamma}(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{i})={\rm Hom}_{{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})}(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{i})
. Similarly, we have
\Psi(\Delta_{i})={\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(P,\Delta_{i})={\rm Hom}_{{\cal F}(\Delta)}(P,\Delta_{i})
. By the preceding considerations, we have an isomorphism of vector spaces

F_{\theta}\Phi(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})=\,_{\theta}{\rm Hom}_{{\cal F}(\Delta^{\prime})}(P^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\Theta^{\prime}}}\,_{\Lambda^{\prime}}{\rm Hom}_{{\cal F}(\Delta)}(P,\Delta_{i})\cong\Psi(\Delta_{i}).

So we only have to verify that
\Theta^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of
\Lambda^{\prime}
-modules. Take any morphism
f:P^{\prime}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Delta^{\prime}_{i}
in
\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
,
\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
p\in P
, then we have

\begin{matrix}\Theta^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime}f)(p)&=&\Theta^{\prime}(\theta(\lambda^{\prime})f)(p)\hfill\\
&=&\Theta^{\prime}(f\circ\Theta(\lambda^{\prime}))(p)\hfill\\
&=&(\Theta^{\prime}(f)\circ\lambda^{\prime})(p)\hfill\\
&=&\Theta^{\prime}(f)(p\lambda^{\prime})=(\lambda^{\prime}\Theta^{\prime}(f))(p).\hfill\\
\end{matrix}

Therefore, if
\Psi^{\prime}:\Lambda^{\prime}\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
is any quasi-inverse of
\Psi
, we have that the composition
\Omega:=\Psi^{\prime}F_{\theta}\Phi:\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
is an equivalence of categories with
\Omega(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})=\Psi^{\prime}F_{\theta}\Phi(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\cong\Delta_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.
From this we obtain the strictness condition for the homological system
{\cal H}
from the corresponding condition for the homological system
{\cal H}^{\prime}
, see (
12.6
).
∎

Now, we can derive the following.

Theorem 12.9
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field and let
\Lambda
be a finite dimensional
k
-algebra with a strict homological system
{\cal H}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta_{i}\}_{i\in{\cal P}})
. Then, the algebra
\Lambda
is Morita equivalent to a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
\Gamma
which admits a strict homological system

{\cal H}^{\prime}=({\cal P},\leq,\{\Delta^{\prime}\}_{i\in P})
such that
\Gamma
has a regular homological exact Borel subalgebra
\underline{A}
, as in (
1.14
). Moreover, there is an equivalence
\Omega:\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
such that
\Omega(\Delta^{\prime}_{i})\cong\Delta_{i}
, for all
i\in{\cal P}
.

Proof.

Consider the
{\cal P}
-strict interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
, adopt the notations of this section.
From (
12.6
) and (
11.10
), the algebra
\underline{A}
is an exact Borel subalgebra of
\Gamma
, the right algebra of
\underline{\cal A}(\Delta)
. From (
11.13
)(4), the Borel subalgebra
\underline{A}
is homological. In order to show that
\underline{A}
is regular, consider the epimorphisms

{\rm Ext}_{\underline{A}}^{1}(S_{i},S_{j})\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Gamma}(\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{i},\Gamma\otimes_{\underline{A}}S_{j})={\rm Ext}_{\Gamma}^{1}(\Delta^{\prime}_{i},\Delta^{\prime}_{j})\cong{\rm Ext}_{\Lambda}^{1}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j}),

for
i,j\in{\cal P}
, where the last isomorphism is obtained from the equivalence
\Omega:\Gamma\hbox{-}{\rm mod}\smash{\mathop{\hbox to20.0pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits}\Lambda\hbox{-}{\rm mod}
constructed in the proof of (
12.8
). Then, since

e_{j}\hat{D}(B)_{0}e_{i}\cong e_{j}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta,\Delta)e_{i}\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})
, we get

\dim_{k}{\rm Ext}_{\underline{A}}^{1}(S_{i},S_{j})=\dim_{k}e_{j}\hat{D}(B)_{0}e_{i}=\dim_{k}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\Lambda}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})
.
∎

Example 12.10
.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra and
{\cal D}=(T,\delta)
its Drozd’s ditalgebra, see
[
3
]
§19 and (23.25). We recall some of its features. Here, we have a splitting
\Lambda=R\oplus J
of the algebra
\Lambda
over its radical
J
, so the algebra
R
is a finite product of copies of the field
k
, say
R=\bigoplus_{i\in F}ke_{i}
, where
1_{R}=\sum_{i\in F}ke_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit element as a sum of primitive orthogonal idempotents of
R
.
Consider the product algebra
S=R\times R
, so we have that
S
is again a finite product of fields and we can write
S=\bigoplus_{i\in{\cal P}}ke_{i}
, with
{\cal P}:={\cal P}^{\prime}\biguplus{\cal P}^{\prime\prime}
, where

{\cal P}^{\prime}=\{i^{\prime}\mid i\in F\}
and
{\cal P}^{\prime\prime}=\{i^{\prime\prime}\mid i\in F\}
are disjoint copies of the set
F
, and
1=\sum_{i\in{\cal P}}e_{i}
is a decomposition of the unit as a sum of primitive central ortogonal idempotents of
S
. Let us fix a basis
\mathbb{B}(i,j)
of the space
e_{j}Je_{i}
, for each
i,i\in F
.
Then, the tensor algebra
T
mentioned above can be identified with the graded path algebra
k({\cal B})
of the bigraph
{\cal B}
defined by the following.
The set of points of
{\cal B}
is
{\cal P}
; and, for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{B}(i,j)
, there is a solid arrow
\alpha:i^{\prime}\rightarrow j^{\prime\prime}
and two dashed arrows
\alpha^{\prime}:i^{\prime}\rightarrow j^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}:i^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow j^{\prime\prime}
in
{\cal B}
.

The ditalgebra
{\cal D}
has no relations, that is we take
I=0
. Consider the preorder
\leq
in
{\cal P}
defined by
i^{\prime}\leq j^{\prime}
and
i^{\prime\prime}\leq j^{\prime\prime}
, for all
i,j\in F
and
i<j
iff
\mathbb{B}(i,j)\not=\emptyset
. Thus, the poset
\overline{\cal P}
has only two elements. Then, the triangular interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{\cal D}=({\cal D},0)
is strict with preordered set
({\cal P},\leq)
and this section applies to it. Its right algebra is not quasi-hereditary in general.

Similarly, given any finite partially ordered sets
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{T}
, the associated ditalgebra
{\cal A}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{T})
, see
[
3
]
(34.1), determines a triangular interlaced weak ditalgebra
\underline{{\cal A}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{T}})=({\cal A}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{T}),0)
, with a preordered set
({\cal P},\leq)
defined similarly.
